Every cancelled run orphans its play (2.107: the pty-forked `ansible-playbook` and the `WorkerProcess` below it are reparented the moment the `ansible-runner` leader dies), and each of those processes becomes a permanent zombie the moment it exits — on the cancel's `KILL` once 2.107 is fixed, or when the play finishes on its own today. A zombie holds no memory or files, only its pid and process-table slot, so this is a slow leak bounded by the container's pid limit rather than an outage, but it grows with every cancel for the container's lifetime and makes `ps` inside the worker lie about what is running. **The test half is fixed** the same day: `playUIDProcessExists` and `playUIDProcessGroups` count only live processes (`isLiveUnderUID` in `internal/e2e/uidboundary/proc_test.go`, which treats state `Z` and `X` as gone), and `reapPlayUIDLeftovers` now fails its test on a live survivor instead of logging it — it had been reporting "survived" for 30 s after a `KILL` that had worked, while the test around it passed. **The production half belongs to 2.107's design**, not a patch of its own: `init: true` in compose (or `tini` in the image) would reap orphans but leave a non-compose deployment unfixed and the play no easier to find; the worker becoming a reaper itself (`PR_SET_CHILD_SUBREAPER` is implied for PID 1; a `SIGCHLD` loop around `wait4(-1)` would race the runner's own `cmd.Wait()`) needs to know which pids are its own; and a per-job subreaper between the worker and `ansible-runner` would both keep a cancelled play's orphans findable by descendancy — the hazard 2.107 names — and reap them. Decide there |
| 2.109 | `TestSetuidHelperSignalModeContract/no_signal_without_the_drop` fails when the runner tests run as root with a C compiler | done | **Fixed 2026-11-02.** Every case whose premise root does not share now skips as root and says why. `no signal without the drop` skips when the effective uid is 0 (root needs no drop to signal). `TestLoadPlayUID/own uid` skips under uid 0, which `zero is root` already covers with its own message. The three read-only-directory cases of renew_writeprobe_test.go call `skipIfStillWritable` after their chmod 0500 and skip when the directory still takes writes. Their old probe ran before the chmod and could never trigger. Checked as root in `golang:1.27.1-alpine` with no network: internal/worker and internal/worker/runner pass, the four cases report SKIP; that image has no gcc, so the setuid case does not run there at all. As an ordinary user nothing changes. Finding: Found 2026-10-29 running the runner package's tests inside `golang:1.27.1-alpine` with `gcc` installed (as root, which is what a container gives you). The case (`internal/worker/runner/setuidhelper_c_test.go`) compiles the setuid helper and runs it as a non-setuid binary expecting the refusal `not setuid-root`; as root, the helper does not refuse, because root needs no privilege drop to signal, so the stated expectation is wrong in that environment. Pre-existing and independent of the play supervisor (it reproduces on `main`); on a developer's machine, and in CI where the tests run as an ordinary user, the case passes. The same root-in-container run also fails `internal/worker`'s `TestLoadPlayUID` (`own_uid`) and the three read-only-directory cases of `renew_writeprobe_test.go`, for the same reason (root ignores the permissions they rely on); all of them pass in the same image under `docker run --user 65534:65534 -e HOME=/tmp -e GOCACHE=/tmp/gocache`, checked 2026-10-30, which is the cheaper way to run the Linux tests until the cases skip as root. **Fix:** skip the case when the test runs as uid 0 and say why in the skip message, or run the helper under an unprivileged uid. Low priority; it only matters to someone running the suite as root in a container |
| 2.110 | `golangci-lint` 2.15.0 reports 26 errcheck findings on `main` that older versions do not | done | **Decided and fixed 2026-11-02: fix the findings, move the pin.** All 26 now handle the received error instead of dropping it. The tests check it (`if err := <-done; err != nil { t.Errorf(...) }`; the two rules ticker tests expect `context.Canceled`, which Ticker.Run returns on purpose). `cmd/manager`'s early-return case puts the error in its failure message. In `internal/agent/loop.go` the scheduler's end is logged as a warning when it carries an error, and the drained receive's error as a debug line. An explicit `_ = <-ch` was tried first and rejected: staticcheck's S1005 flags it, so errcheck and staticcheck agree only on real handling. `golangci-lint run --max-issues-per-linter 0 --max-same-issues 0` reports 0 issues with 2.15.0, and ci.yml's pin moves to v2.15.0 (its stale v2.13.2 comment corrected). Finding: Found 2026-10-29: `golangci-lint` 2.15.0 (the version installed locally; `go1.27.3` toolchain) reports **26** `errcheck` findings on `main` — 13 in `internal/worker/loop_test.go`, 8 in `internal/agent/checks/scheduler_test.go`, 2 in `internal/rules/ticker_test.go`, 1 in `cmd/manager/main_test.go` (mostly `<-done` or `<-errC` on a `chan error`) and 2 non-test ones, `internal/agent/loop.go:239` and `:373` — where older versions reported none. (Run with `--max-issues-per-linter 0 --max-same-issues 0`: the default caps hide all but a few.) CI pins `v2.14.0` (`.github/workflows/ci.yml`; its comment above the step still says v2.13.2), so CI is green and the findings only show to someone running a newer linter locally; the branch `fix/2.107-cancel-stops-the-play` is gated as "no findings beyond these 26". **Needs a decision**, not a quick edit: either fix the 26 (receive into `_` or assert the error in the tests, handle it in `internal/agent/loop.go`) so the repo is clean under both versions, or keep the pin and write down that a newer linter reports them. Whichever is chosen, the day CI's pin moves the findings arrive all at once |
| 2.111 | The embedded worker's container has no init | done | **Closed 2026-10-30:** `deploy/compose/docker-compose.yml` gives the `manager` service `init: true` too, `deploy/compose/README.md`'s embedded worker section says why and asks for `--init` on a plain `docker run`, and the docs' built-in worker section (noix-docs `pulsix` 2d194bc) says the same. Found 2026-10-29 closing 2.108. `deploy/compose/docker-compose.yml` gives the `worker` service `init: true`, but not the `manager` service, which hosts the embedded worker when `PULSIX_EMBEDDED_WORKER=1` (off by default; `internal/app/embedded_worker.go`). There the manager binary is PID 1, so the embedded worker keeps 2.108's residual orphan paths: a repository sync's `ssh` outliving its context-killed `git`, and the tree of a play supervisor the worker had to `KILL` as its backstop. They become zombies for the container's lifetime. A play's own orphans are not affected: the per-run supervisor (2.107) reaps them for the embedded worker too, since `cmd/manager` dispatches the supervisor argument. **Fix:** `init: true` on `manager` (it changes nothing else for the manager) and a line in the admin docs' built-in worker section, or decide the embedded worker is trusted-single-tenant-only enough not to matter. Low priority while the embedded worker stays off by default |
| 2.112 | An unsupported host shows only at the first playbook job, not at worker start | done | **Closed 2026-10-30:** `cmd/worker` calls `supervisor.Probe` right after loading its configuration whenever it will run plays under the supervisor (`Config.PlaySupervisor` set, i.e. on Linux), and refuses to start as a configuration error (exit 2) with the probe's refusal in its log. The probe (`newSystemWith`, `internal/worker/supervisor/system_linux.go`) now checks both calls: it opens a pidfd on itself and sends it signal 0, since a seccomp profile can allow `pidfd_open` and block `pidfd_send_signal`, which the supervisor would otherwise first need at a cancel. Refusing rather than warning follows `worker.Load`'s own rule that a worker which cannot supervise must not look healthy; it costs such a worker its connection tests, which it could still run. Left: the embedded worker is not probed (the manager image has no Ansible runtime, so it runs no plays, and the manager must not refuse to start over it), and nothing reports the host to the manager, so the operator sees an offline worker and its log, not a reason in the GUI. End-user docs: noix-docs `pulsix` ebdf69a. Found 2026-10-29 in the whole-branch review of 2.107. The play supervisor needs pidfds (`pidfd_open`, `pidfd_send_signal`): a kernel 5.3 or newer, and a container runtime whose seccomp profile allows them (Docker's default profile allows them from 20.10). Without them the supervisor refuses, by name, and every playbook job on that worker fails with that refusal as its error. Nothing fails earlier: the worker enrolls, connects, reports itself healthy and claims jobs. **Planned:** probe at worker start (open a pidfd on the worker itself, as `newSystemWith` does in `internal/worker/supervisor/system_linux.go`) and make an unsupported host show at boot, in the worker's log and ideally in what it reports to the manager, so an operator sees it before the first job. The host requirement is in the end-user installation docs from this branch on |
| 2.113 | A version-pinned worker keeps the old Cancel while the run view stops warning | done | **Closed 2026-10-30, as a capability rather than a version comparison:** the worker says on every claim whether a cancel stops the play on it (`ClaimJobRequest.cancel_stops_play`, true exactly when it runs plays under the play supervisor, so a `dev` build or a worker off Linux answers truthfully), and the `ClaimJob` UPDATE records the answer on the job (`jobs.cancel_stops_play`, migration 00072). Recording it at claim time answers the second open point: a worker upgraded after the run cannot change what its run says. NULL (never claimed, claimed in process or before the migration, or cleared by the reaper's and a revocation's requeue, whose first worker's play may still be running, see 2.57) reads as false. The run detail returns it as `cancel_stops_play`, and the run view shows the pre-2.107 warning about every host whenever it is not true, the interrupted-host warning only when it is. A cancelled run that was never claimed therefore also gets the stronger warning, as it did before 2.107; telling that case apart by `started_at` would hide a requeued run whose first play may still run. End-user docs: noix-docs `pulsix` dbc3a65. Found 2026-10-29 in the whole-branch review of 2.107. The Add worker dialog's snippets (`workerSnippets`, `internal/api/worker_snippets.go`) pin `noixdev/pulsix-worker:<manager version at enrollment>`. After a manager upgrade, a worker added that way keeps its old image, and with it the old Cancel: the job reports `cancelled` while the play keeps running. The run view no longer warns about that (2.107 took the warning back to "a host was interrupted"), and the docs say Cancel stops the run. The docs now say Cancel stops the play only on workers at this release or newer, and to update pinned worker images after upgrading; that is a sentence an operator has to find. **Decision to make:** gate the run view's cancelled-run wording on the version the worker already reports to the manager (the worker sends its build version on every claim, and the manager stores it on the worker): a run cancelled on a worker older than the 2.107 release would keep the old, stronger warning. Open points: where the cut-over version lives (one constant beside the run view, or a capability the worker reports instead of a version comparison, which also covers `dev` builds), and whether the job row has to record the worker's version at claim time, since a worker can be upgraded between the run and the moment someone reads it |
| 2.114 | Loose ends the cancel-stops-the-play whole-branch review deferred | done | **Closed 2026-11-02: two fixed, the rest kept as records.** Fixed: `parseArgs` refuses a repeated `--grace` like every other deviation (TestParseArgsRefuses/repeated grace), and the runner package's new TestMain removes the play supervisor its Linux tests build (checked in a Linux container: no `pulsix-play-supervisor-*` dir left). Kept as they are, each a choice rather than a defect: a pid first seen in the last TERM pass gets only the remaining grace before KILL, because the grace bounds the whole stop; the backstop is the point. `raise` stays in `system_*.go` beside the other platform calls. The routing tests' one stderr line and the session-escape fake are noted in the tests. `cancelRun`'s duplication and the hand-mirrored `stopGrace` stay until those tests change for another reason. Acceptance 4's exit-4 and extra-vars cases stay covered by TestSupervisedExitCodesPassThrough and `summarize`'s unit tests. `TestLinuxSystemFindsOrphanedGrandchild`'s waitFor has not flaked in the runs since (watch item). Finding: Collected so they survive the plan's scratch directory (spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-29-cancel-stops-the-play-design.md`). None changes behaviour a user can see. **`parseArgs` accepts a repeated `--grace`** (the last wins) where it refuses every other deviation; the worker is its only caller and never repeats it. **Pids first seen in the last `TERM` pass are `KILL`ed without a full grace**: `stopTree`'s `TERM` phase ends at the deadline, so a process forked moments before it gets only the remaining slice. **`raise` lives in `system_*.go`** rather than a `raise_*.go` of its own. **`TestLinuxSystemFindsOrphanedGrandchild`'s `waitFor` closure can `t.Fatal`** if the `sleep` exits mid-poll; watch it for flakes. **The routing tests print one supervisor refusal line to real stderr.** **The escape fake is a session escape, not a double fork** (`writeFakeRunner`'s ESCAPE and DAEMON modes); the in-image tests cover the real nested groups. **The Linux runner tests leave the built supervisor binary in `os.MkdirTemp`** (`playSupervisor`, `supervised_linux_test.go`; the package has no `TestMain` to clean up). **`cancelRun` duplicates the harness inlined in `TestCancellingAPlayUnderThePlayUIDReportsCancelled` and `...StopsIt`**, and the in-image `stopGrace` mirrors `defaultKillGrace` by hand. **Acceptance 4's exit-4 and extra-vars cases have no in-image test** (they are covered by `TestSupervisedExitCodesPassThrough` and `summarize`'s unit tests) |
| 2.115 | The worker logs `skipping malformed runner event … invalid character 'W'` in most in-image runs | done | **Fixed 2026-11-02.** The warning now carries a bounded, redacted prefix of the line (`line_prefix`, at most 120 bytes; redacted before the cut so a secret cannot straddle it). That named the line at once: ansible-core's `[WARNING]: Failed to create the directory PosixPath('<work dir>/home/.ansible'): [Errno 2] No such file or directory`, printed to stdout. The worker points HOME at `<work dir>/home`, but only repository-backed runs ever created that directory (for the fetch's ssh_config). `materializeRunnerConfig` now creates it for every run, 0700 like the rest of the work dir until ShareWorkDir opens it to the play's group. In the worker image the in-image suite went from 12 such warnings to none. Tests: TestMalformedRunnerLineIsNamedRedacted, TestRunnerConfigCreatesTheRunsHome, and TestLocalhostPlayRunsUnderThePlayUID now fails if a run prints a line that is not an event. Finding: Found 2026-10-29 building 2.107, and pre-existing: it appeared in the first in-image run on `main` (`b19c794`) before any of that work. `streamEvents` (`internal/worker/runner/runner.go`) warns once per stdout line of `ansible-runner` that is not a JSON event; the offending line starts with `W`, and what writes it has not been looked at (a warning printed to stdout instead of stderr is the likely shape). It changes no status and no assertion, but a warning in every run teaches an operator to ignore the worker's warnings. **To do:** capture the line itself (log a bounded, redacted prefix of it instead of only the decode error), find what writes it, and either keep it off stdout or recognise and drop it quietly |
| 2.116 | Assistant access: an MCP endpoint for AI assistants, assistant tokens with areas, a per-organization switch and run requests a person approves | done | Spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-16-assistant-access-design.md`, plan `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-16-assistant-access.md`. `/api/mcp` serves MCP over stateless Streamable HTTP; each tool dispatches in-process to a fixed REST route, so authorization, validation, masking and audit stay the REST layer's. Assistant tokens (`api_tokens.kind = assistant`, `areas`) work only through that dispatch and only within their areas; the org switch is off by default. The assistant never starts a run: `run_requests` are approved in an interactive session against a fingerprint of the run picture the person saw, and expire after an hour. Audit entries now name the token. User docs: noix-docs `docs/pulsix-assistant-access`. | 2.127 | Loose ends the 2.126 work left | done | From 2026-11-24; done 2026-11-26 on `fix/2.127-loose-ends-race` (plan `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-26-withdrawn-claim-race-2.127.md`). (1) not done: the picture lists each targeted zone's full host list, which is what the claim renders, so a host the template's limit excludes still shows when it shares a zone with a selected host (the picture does show the limit). Host names are reachable (the request-limit narrowing already matches them in `runrequests.narrowTargets`), but the same target list is what the vars digest binds and what the enrollment list and the only-host check come from: narrowing it per host either drops from the approval's binding the vars of hosts the claim still renders and ships (a play can read them through `hostvars`), a security trade-off for the owner, or needs the picture to carry two host lists (rendered and played) through digest, enrollment and only-host check; it would also change every limited template's fingerprint once more after rc.6; (2) ~~the withdrawn job reads as an extra `error` row~~ done 2026-11-24 (`fix/2.127-2.129-small-loose-ends`): job DTOs carry `withdrawn_at_claim` and the SPA shows such a job as "Not run — approval withdrawn" in the run view, its group section and the runs list; (3) not done: a snapshot builds the renderer's indexes twice (`render.Carries` and `render.Render`); sharing one renderer would carry a group index built before the reveal replaces the vars and would replace the `Renderer.carries` seam the 2.126 equivalence tests swap, for microseconds next to a snapshot's reads and decryptions; (4) ~~the badge's fallback asks the count route on every poll~~ done 2026-11-24: a 404 is remembered per organization for ten minutes; (5) done: a claim the guard refuses as withdrawn is failed only after `ClaimGuard.ConfirmWithdrawn` re-reads the request `FOR SHARE` inside the failing transaction (`jobs.guardClaim`, `runrequests.Service.ConfirmWithdrawn`). A re-approval's decision waits for that transaction, so its replay sees the job failed and refills the zone; a confirmation that waited for a re-approval reads it approved, writes nothing, and the guard checks the job again, which then runs; a request that flips more than three times within one claim fails the job as an ordinary refusal. Both orders are pinned against the real database (`internal/api/run_request_reapproval_race_test.go`, `internal/runrequests/store_sql_lock_test.go`). No migration, API or protocol change |

| 2.135 | A worker whose clock lags could reuse an event sequence number | done | Done 2026-11-26 (`fix/2.134-2.135-vault-names-event-seq`): the append path bounds its `max(seq)` read at `created_at` − `EventClockSlack`, the same as the log read. Lag beyond the slack: 2.138. Originally: Found 2026-11-26 in the 2.133 log-search work: the append path reads `max(seq)` of a job's events bounded below strictly by the job's `created_at` (backlog 2.50's chunk pruning), but a worker whose clock runs behind stamps events before `created_at`, so those events fall outside the bound and the next append could pick a sequence number already used. The read side (2.133) reads from `created_at` − 1 h for the same reason. Fix: the same slack on the append path's bound, or derive the bound from the earliest event the job has |
| 2.138 | An event a worker stamps more than an hour before its job could still reuse a sequence number | done | Done 2026-11-26 (`fix/1.54-2.138-tabs-event-clamp`): the append clamps an event's stored `at` to no earlier than `created_at` − `EventClockSlack`, keeping the worker's own time as `worker_at` in object payloads. Originally: From the 2.134/2.135 final review: 2.135 bounds the append's `max(seq)` read at the job's `created_at` minus one hour (the read side's slack), so a worker clock lagging by more than that still slips past the bound — a reused seq and an event hidden from the log view. Fix: clamp an event's stored `at` to at least `created_at` − slack on append (keeping the worker's own time in the payload) |
| 2.139 | Webhook mappings are not checked against secret names | done | Done 2026-11-26 (`fix/2.139-webhook-mapping-secret-shadows`): the run-request check moved to `internal/secretshadow`; a `template_run` trigger's mapping targets are checked against the secrets of the template's hosts (scope before any limit), its playbook and its repository's vault on save (400) and again at every delivery after the mapping is applied (422 at `extra_vars.<name>`, nothing started or collected) and when a settle delay's collected run is due (not started, deliveries failed). Originally: From the 2.134 final review: run requests refuse an override key that is a secret anywhere the run takes values from (inventory secrets, playbook-declared secrets, vault-encrypted repository variables — 2.130/2.134), but a webhook trigger's mapping targets (`internal/triggers/validate.go`) are only checked against the execution-level name rule; on a template without an extra-vars schema a mapping may target any valid name, so an external sender could replace a secret's value. Apply the same secret-shadow check to mapping targets on save and at delivery |
| 2.137 | Webhook triggers wait for a quiet moment before they run (debounce) | done | Done 2026-11-26 (`feat/2.137-trigger-settle-delay`, owner decisions: last delivery's values; quiet window plus maximum wait, default 10 × settle; an active run of the trigger first finishes, then one run): `settle_seconds`/`max_wait_seconds` per trigger (migration 00133), deliveries collected into one pending run (outcome `collected`), a manager ticker starts it exactly once (idempotency key for template runs, queued-sync dedupe for repository syncs), Run now / Discard, API, MCP, SPA and the trigger request picture. Originally: Owner idea 2026-11-26: a tool that sends the same hook 10 times in 5 minutes should start one run, not ten. Today a trigger has only `on_active` (`queue`: every delivery queues a run; `skip`: drops deliveries while a run is active, implemented for repository sync only) and a rate limit that refuses deliveries beyond it (429), so nothing collects. Wanted: a per-trigger **settle delay** (e.g. 0 = off, 30 s … 30 min): a delivery opens or extends a waiting window; the run starts once no further delivery arrived for that long, plus a **maximum wait** so a steady stream still runs at least every N minutes. Which delivery's mapped values the run gets (the last one, by default) and how the delivery log shows collapsed deliveries ("collapsed into run X") need a design round; also `skip` for template triggers and how a waiting window interacts with a run already active |
| 2.136 | Inventory values an assistant writes are plain text, and they pause what runs unattended | done | Done 2026-11-26 (`feat/assistant-inventory-values-untrusted`, owner decisions D6 and D7): values, field defaults and schema names an assistant writes are marked and reach runs inert until a person changes them or accepts them (session-only accept routes; a resent value keeps its mark); an assistant's change to inventory values, schema, memberships, group position or name, host creation, a host's jump host, SSH settings or zone pauses the schedules and disables the triggers whose runs it reaches (by scope, along follow-ups), with review requests; host addresses must be an IP or a host name, `ansible_host` is always inert, an assistant cannot move a managed host's connection, and its password connection tests need a pinned host key. Migration 00131. Originally: Found 2026-11-26 in the 2.133 final review: an assistant with inventory write (`set_group_vars`, `add_group_member`, `set_member_vars`, `set_group_schema` defaults) can put Jinja such as `lookup('pipe', …)` into values a scheduled or triggered run renders, without any approval. Owner decision D6 (both): mark assistant-written inventory values untrusted (inert in the rendered inventory; a person's save clears the mark) and pause schedules / disable triggers whose runs receive changed values, with a review request as in 2.132. Host membership changes stay excluded |
| 2.140 | The in-image cancel tests of `TestPlaybookUIDBoundary` lost their cancel under load | done | Done 2026-11-26 on `fix/e2e-flakes-under-load`. Cause: the cancel tests' "the play has started" wait (`playUIDProcessExists`) counted any live process of the play uid except the supervisor, and the loop's startup sweep (`Loop.Run`, before its first claim) also runs as the play uid. A poll that caught the sweep, or caught it exiting (a Go leader in teardown still counts as live, and its cmdline already reads empty), released the wait before the job was claimed; the fake manager's `cancel` found no run event stream and returned silently, the play slept on, and the test failed 30 s later with "the job was not reported within 30s of cancelling". Reproduced in the image under CPU load: 5 of 20 runs, every one released by `__pulsix-play-sweep` with the cancel dropped; none idle. Fix (test only, no product change): the wait also requires the run's event stream to be open (`playStarted`), which the loop opens only after the startup sweep is reaped; `isWorkerOwnCmdline` leaves the sweep out next to the supervisor; a cancel before the stream exists now fails at once instead of being dropped. 100 of 100 runs passed under the same load. The same day's `TestRulePipelineGate` failure (99.59 s, "last state unknown") was not load: the power log shows a clamshell sleep from 14:32:46 to 16:07:56, broken only by short maintenance wakes, during that run; Go's monotonic clock on macOS stops in sleep while the rule engine judges staleness on wall time, so the tuple read unknown on every wake. Three runs under -race and CPU load on all cores passed. No change there |
| 2.141 | The claim guard does not compare a run's frozen host list with the approved picture | done | From 2026-11-26 (review of 2.127, an older Low gap); done 2026-11-27 on `fix/2.127-loose-ends-race` (plan addendum in `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-26-withdrawn-claim-race-2.127.md`). A job stores its zone's host list at enqueue, and the claim renders that list; the claim guard (`runrequests.Service.CheckRunClaim`) recomputes the picture from the live inventory and compares its fingerprint, but never compares the job's frozen host list with it. A host that joins a targeted zone between the approval's picture read and the enqueue, and leaves the scope again before the claim, is therefore not in the approved picture nor in the live one, so the fingerprints match, yet the job renders it: its vars ship to the worker without having been approved, and with a request's limit override that matches it the play also runs on it. The window is the time between the approval's read and the enqueue plus a later departure before the claim, so it needs two inventory changes at the right moments. A fix would have the guard compare each job's host ids with the picture's targets (or the zone lists they come from), or freeze the approved host list with the decision and enqueue exactly it. Fixed: the claim hands the job's hosts to the guard (`jobs.ApprovedRun.HostIDs`), and `CheckRunClaim`, once the live picture's fingerprint matched the approved one (so its targets are the approved targets), refuses any job host outside them with `ErrHostsChangedAfterApproval` ("the run's hosts changed after it was approved"), an ordinary failure of the run; fewer hosts than approved pass, runs without a request are unaffected. Nothing new is stored, no migration. Pinned by `TestRunRequestClaimRefusesAHostTheApprovalDidNotInclude` (a trigger makes a host join inside the approval's decision). Every request path keeps job hosts within the targets otherwise (a limit override narrows the jobs through `OnlyHosts`), so the check refuses only the race. Upgrade note in `docs/upgrades.md` |
| 2.142 | A re-approval never runs a host that joined a zone the first approval already covered | idea | From 2026-11-27 (review of 2.141, info 3; pre-existing). When an approval's enqueue fails partway and the request is approved again, the replay of the run group (`jobs.reserveRunGroup`) counts every job of the first attempt that may have run as covering its zone, and enqueues only the zones nothing covers. A host that joined a covered zone between the two approvals is in the second approval's picture, but the old job froze the first attempt's host list, and the claim check of 2.141 lets it through with fewer hosts than approved: that host never runs, and nothing says so. Same coverage semantics as the withdrawn-job refill of 2.126/2.127. Options: refill per host instead of per zone (a new job for the zone's missing hosts, which then runs the play twice in that zone, once per job), or refuse a re-approval whose picture adds a host to a covered zone and ask for a new request. No fix yet |
| 2.143 | Follow-up runs an approved request starts are not checked at their claim | idea | From 2026-11-27 (review of 2.141, info 4). The follow-ups an approval starts (backlog 2.122) run in run groups of their own, which no request owns, so `runrequests.CheckRunClaim` lets their claims through without any comparison with what the person approved: the picture's follow-ups block names the follow-up templates, but neither their hosts nor their vars are bound, and a host or value that changes between the approval and a follow-up's claim runs unreviewed. 2.122 already records the narrower case the owner chose to document (a follow-up template edited after the approved run's claim starts with what it holds then); this row is the broader question whether an approval should bind its follow-ups' hosts and values at all, which is the owner's. No fix yet |
| 2.144 | `TestAlertResolveNotificationEndToEnd` sometimes sees no resolve message within 30 s under load | planned | From 2026-11-27. The test (`internal/e2e/alertresolve_test.go`) failed twice on heavily loaded machines (other suites running in parallel) with `the webhook received no "closed" item for alert … in 30s; 1 request(s) in all`, and passed in 17 later runs. The opened message arrived, the alert closed, then nothing reached the test's webhook receiver within 30 s; the manager log of that run held no dispatcher lines. Hypothesis (unproven): the resolve delivery's first attempt failed under load (timeout or connection error) and the dispatcher's first retry comes after exactly 30 s (`retryDelay` in `internal/notify/dispatch.go`), the same as the test's wait. Test change only so far: when either the "opened" or the "closed" wait times out, the test now prints the org's `notification_deliveries` rows (status, attempts, next attempt, `locked_until`, last error, created/sent times) and a summary of the receiver's requests before failing; the waits and the retry delay are unchanged. The fix (waiting past one retry, or a shorter first retry) follows once a failing run shows the cause: `attempts=1` with a last error and `next_attempt_at` 30 s out confirms the hypothesis, a pending row with `attempts=0` points at the dispatcher not claiming it instead |
| 2.97 | Go and the browser reject the same vault labels but for different reasons on U+0085 and U+FEFF | done | `vaultlabel.Invalid` and `invalidVaultLabel` now share one merged message for both the trim check and the Cc/Cf check (a tagless-switch multi-value case in Go, an `||` in TypeScript), so the report depends only on whether a label is rejected, never on which half of the merged check — or which language's trim() — did the rejecting. A new test in each pins that NEL and the BOM, at every position, produce byte-for-byte the same message |
| 2.98 | `proto/buf.yaml`'s `breaking: use: WIRE_JSON` drops source-level checks for every other message in the package | done | Revisited and kept: recorded directly in `buf.yaml`'s own comment, next to the original decision, so the next reader finds the reasoning without a backlog row. The proto is still one service and a dozen messages a reviewer reads in full for any change; worth a second look once that stops being true, not before. No code change |
| 2.99 | `alert_routes.sql`'s replace comment says the DELETE commits before the INSERT, inside a transaction where it does not | done | Both comments (`DeleteRouteSeverities` and `InsertRouteSeverities`) now say what the design actually guarantees: the DELETE is *ordered* before the INSERT (why two statements, not one data-modifying `WITH`), but the two commit together when `replaceSelections`' own transaction does — not that either commits first. Comment-only; no behavior change, no test |
| 2.100 | A shared check definition's params editor only covers the `service` plugin's unit list | superseded | Superseded by 2.101, which names this exact gap (a shared `disk` check's `mounts` editor) as one of the two editors it covers, plus the `extra_vars`/`extra_vars_schema` editor this row did not mention. Closed here rather than tracked in two rows for one piece of work |
| 2.101 | `SharedResourcesPage.tsx`'s dialogs have no editor for a shared template's extra vars or a shared `disk` check's mounts | done | v0.2f whole-branch review, and 2.100 folded in. A prior fix made both dialogs ROUND-TRIP the stored value through an edit that does not touch it, closing the silent-data-loss bug; neither was EDITABLE, so an instance admin had to set these through the API and then never open that definition in the GUI again without re-sending them by hand. The docs already described both as part of what a shared definition carries — the GUI was the thing behind. **Templates:** `extra_vars_schema` through `SchemaBuilder` (nested, the compact form `TemplateDialog` already uses) and `extra_vars` through `SchemaForm`, seeded from the stored values. `SchemaBuilder` gained an optional `types` prop so the shared dialog can withhold `secret`: the server refuses a secret-typed field on a shared definition by construction (`schemaHasSecretField` — the values are written into every organization's own column unsealed), and offering a type whose save can never succeed is worse than not offering it. **Checks:** a `mounts` textarea for the `disk` plugin, same shape as the existing `service` units list, with `CheckDialog`'s own contract that an empty list means every mount. **A finding beyond the row:** `validateTemplateDefinitionInput` ran only `ansiblevars.ForbiddenIn`, which looks at top-level names — the same gap 2.71 closed for an organization's own templates, and worse here, because a shared definition's extra vars are written straight into EVERY organization's `job_templates.extra_vars`: one malformed `__ansible` marker would break a run in each of them with a parse error in a document none of their operators wrote. `CheckMarkers` now runs there too. Docs: `admin/shared-resources.md` gained the secret-field rule, which was never stated |
| 2.102 | `ListCredentialUsage` never counted the labelled vault identity link tables | done | Found during the 2.76/2.92/2.94/2.97/2.99 triage, not named by an existing row. The query counted `git_repos.credential_id`/`.vault_credential_id` but never `git_repo_vault_credentials` or `job_template_vault_credentials` (migration 00059) — the exact class of gap the query's own comment already describes closing, reopened one layer down by v0.2e's labelled identities. A vault credential used only through a labelled identity read as unused in the GUI and then refused to delete on the link tables' own foreign keys (`store_sql.go` already mapped that violation to `ErrInUse` — the count was what lied, not the delete). `Repos` now folds in `git_repo_vault_credentials`, deduplicated by repository (a repository carrying both the legacy slot and a matching labelled identity for the same credential — the normal shape for anything saved before 2.92 — counts once); a new `Templates` field counts `job_template_vault_credentials` separately, since a template-only reference is not a repository one |
| 2.103 | `agent.LoadIdentity` reports "absent" and "corrupt" identically, and `serialOf` prints the result as `<nil>` | done | Found 2026-10-22 triaging an `internal/e2e` failure; closed 2026-10-23, and the underlying race the row listed as unestablished turned out to be the actual cause. **(a) and (b):** `LoadIdentity` keeps collapsing "the files are not there" and "they are there and do not parse" into `(nil, nil)` — the enrollment path depends on a corrupt identity re-enrolling rather than wedging the agent — and `LoadIdentityDetail` now returns the same thing plus a reason for a human, `""` when the identity is usable. The reason is explicitly not for branching on. **The product side, which the row did not name:** `Loop.Run` logged `"no valid agent identity; enrollment path"` identically on a first start and on a truncated certificate file, so an operator watching an agent re-enrol repeatedly had no signal at all; it now carries the reason, including the third case `LoadIdentityDetail` does not speak to (an identity that loaded and is simply no longer valid). **The race, now ESTABLISHED:** `agentapi`'s `Service.Enroll` calls `Agents.Register` INSIDE the RPC, before it returns the signed certificate, so the manager lists the agent strictly before the agent has the response — let alone before `SaveEnrollment` has written its four files. `waitForAgent` was therefore satisfied while the data dir was still empty, and `serialOf`'s single read found nothing. That is exactly how `TestAgentEnrollmentGate` failed under the parallel group and passed alone. `serialOf` now waits, bounded, and reports the last reason if the directory never fills. The four-writes-not-atomic-together observation stands but was not needed: the window that bit was the one BEFORE the first write. The renewal path remains ruled out |

## v0.3 · Agent & monitoring

| # | Item | Status | Notes |
|---|------|--------|-------|
| 3.1 | Agent binary: Linux amd64/arm64 (systemd) | done | v0.3a: `cmd/agent` + `internal/agent` — first-start enrollment, one mTLS stream, heartbeat 30 s, facts at connect and every 6 h, 1 s→60 s reconnect backoff; `make agent-binaries` cross-compiles both arches into the manager's embedded dist |
| 3.2 | Agent binary: Windows service | done | **Done 2026-11-12, spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-12-windows-macos-agents-design.md`.** `install.ps1` (`GET /install.ps1`, shown in the Add-agent dialog as `install_command_windows`) installs the `PulsixAgent` service (delayed automatic start, restart after 5 s) with the binary under `%ProgramFiles%\Pulsix` and identity, token and log under `%ProgramData%\Pulsix`; it needs PowerShell as administrator. A pre-existing data folder not owned by SYSTEM or Administrators, or holding an entry with another owner or extra access, is refused by script and agent alike (the agent checks at every start) |
| 3.3 | Agent binary: macOS launchd | done | **Done 2026-11-12, spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-12-windows-macos-agents-design.md`.** The existing `install.sh` installs a launchd daemon `dev.noix.pulsix-agent` (binary `/usr/local/bin/pulsix-agent`, data `/Library/Application Support/Pulsix`, log `/Library/Logs/Pulsix/agent.log`); the command is the Linux one. Binaries for darwin and windows, amd64 and arm64, ship in the manager image. Unsigned: see 3.48 |
| 3.4 | Enrollment: one-time token, install script, mTLS cert issued by manager CA | done | v0.3a: `pxa1` tokens (bound to a host or creating one, 1–100 uses, expiry), `GET /install.sh` templated with the manager's public URL + `GET /agents/bin/{os}/{arch}` with a sha256 beside it; the token carries the CA fingerprint the agent pins its first connection against; 90-day certificate renewed at 2/3 lifetime. Agents dial the MANAGER directly, not a zone worker — the relay is v0.6 (spec §8 delta) |
| 3.4a | E2E: agent enrollment, monitoring pipeline (threshold → alert → notify → resolve), remote checks | done | **Status corrected 2026-11-13** (3.x backlog audit). Enrollment, measurement, threshold → state → resolve and alert → notify are covered end to end; the two parts not covered moved to 3.4b. Was: milestone gate — three of four parts done. Enrollment in v0.3a (`internal/e2e/agent_test.go`: both token flows, facts + auto tags, renewal, revocation; `web/e2e/agent.spec.ts`: the operator journey). The **measurement** pipeline in v0.3b-1 (`internal/e2e/checks_test.go`: the seed set reaches an enrolled agent, an edited interval re-times the running agent fast enough that its enrolment-time configuration could not have produced the readings observed, those readings appear as current values and as graph points, and a monitoring group's definition beats the organization's for a tagged host; it asserts on `cpu` only, never on `service`, because the container provides no systemd). The **threshold → state → resolve** half in v0.3b-2 (`internal/e2e/rules_test.go`: a host-bound check of its own reaches `warn` the instant its threshold is crossed, `crit` only once the deterioration has held for the rule's own `for_duration`, `ok` again the moment a recovering value applies — no ripening on the way down — and the agent falling silent altogether reaches `unknown` once three of its check's own intervals have passed; `web/e2e/fleet.spec.ts` puts one host in each of the four states on the fleet page and filters down to just them). What "alert" meant when this row's title was written is now `warn`/`crit` — a judged state, not yet a notification. Still missing: **notify**, which needs v0.4's channels, and remote checks |
| 3.4b | E2E: synthetic (server-run) checks, and the notification sent when an alert resolves | done | Done 2026-11-24 on `fix/small-items-3.60-3.68-3.4b` (`9884036a`, `c74af797`, `62deb575`): `TestAlertResolveNotificationEndToEnd` (an alert routed to a test webhook sends `opened`, then `closed` as a separate delivery) and `TestSyntheticCheckEndToEnd` (an http check from the manager's own probe runner against a test server: ok, crit on failure, ok again). The second needed test seams `app.WithProbeGuard`/`WithProbeTick`/`WithProbeRefresh` (the manager's guard refuses loopback; `cmd/manager` never passes them). Originally: Split from 3.4a on 2026-11-13: no end-to-end test drives a synthetic check from definition to state, and none asserts the resolve notification. |
| 3.5 | Agent local config `agent.yaml`: secrets by name | done | **Done 2026-11-12** (branch `feat/3.5-agent-secrets`). The agent reads named secrets from `agent.yaml` in its data directory (`/etc/pulsix`, `/Library/Application Support/Pulsix`, `%ProgramData%\Pulsix`; `--config` overrides; optional) with `internal/agent/secrets`: only a top-level `secrets:` map, names and fields `[A-Za-z0-9_.-]` 1-64 chars, string values, no YAML tags other than `!!str`, anchors, merge keys, multiple documents or nulls, at most 1 MiB, 256 secrets, 4 KiB per value. The file passes the agent.env permission rule on every read (root-owned and closed to group and others on Unix, symlinks refused; SYSTEM or Administrators only on Windows; the Windows start-up check of every data-directory entry, and the installer's, skip it) and is re-read every minute; a broken or too-open file drops all secrets, and a parse error names a line and never quotes a value. The heartbeat carries the secret names and the file state, never a value (`secret_names`, `secrets_state`); the manager stores them with the agent (migration 00086, bounded again on arrival), the host page shows them, and an agent with no report is shown as unknown. Check plugins receive their secret through the shared `secret` parameter (`secretName` format, `internal/agent/checks/secret.go`). User docs: Agent configuration in noix-docs. Static local checks moved to 3.5a |
| 3.5a | Static local checks defined in `agent.yaml` | done | **Done 2026-11-14** (branch `feat/3.5a-local-checks`). Spec: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-14-local-checks-design.md`. A top-level `checks:` key in `agent.yaml` (same file, permission rule and reload as `secrets:` and `commands:`) defines checks on the host: key `local-…`, `plugin`, `interval_seconds` (10-86400, default 60), `params`. The agent validates them against the plugin descriptors, refuses one bad entry with its line and keeps the rest, runs them next to the manager's at start without the manager, and re-reads the file within about a minute. A local key equal to an enabled manager check is shadowed and reported. The heartbeat carries the running checks and the problems; the manager re-validates (migration 00095, nullable `host_agents.local_checks` / `local_check_problems`), resolves them after the host's other checks, and the host page shows them as "Defined on the host", read-only; rules judge them. New check definitions with a `local-` key are refused. User docs: Agent configuration and Monitoring in noix-docs |
| 3.6 | Check definitions pushed from GUI to agent, secret references by name | done | v0.3b-1 shipped the push half: an operator's edit in the GUI resolves that host's set, stamps it with a version and pushes it over the open agent stream, and every heartbeat carries the version the agent is running so a missed push heals on the next one (`internal/checks` resolver, `internal/agentapi` delivery, `internal/agent/checks` scheduler). **Secret half done with 3.5 (2026-11-12):** a check parameter references a secret by name (the shared `secret` parameter, validated by the `secretName` format) and the agent resolves it from its local `agent.yaml`, so the value is never in the manager. A missing name shows "Secret … is not defined in agent.yaml on this host." on the check and a collection error. No plugin uses the parameter yet; the database checks will be the first |
| 3.6a | Check engine: plugins in code with declared parameter schema | done | **Done 2026-11-14:** delivered by 3.54 (`internal/checkplugins`), which declares every plugin's parameters, metrics and location in one descriptor the GUI and the validator both read. |
| 3.6b | Check definitions as data: plugin + params + interval + binding (host / monitoring group / org) | done | **Status corrected 2026-11-13** (3.x backlog audit). The model, bindings (host, monitoring group, organization), resolution and GUI ship; the plugins the row waited for exist (3.2, 3.3, 3.9–3.13). Only the preset feature is left: 3.6i. Was: v0.3b-1: `check_definitions` (migration 00043) with key, plugin, params, interval, enabled, origin and a binding to the organization, a monitoring group (`monitoring_groups`, migration 00042 — a named, saved host-tag selector with a priority) or a host; resolution is per key with host > group > org, group priority then age breaking ties, and the host page names the winning scope. Both the definitions and the groups are editable in the GUI. What is missing: only **Linux base** is seeded (four rows: cpu, memory, disk, service) — "PostgreSQL", "Windows base", "macOS base", "Web server", "Docker host", "Endpoint" need their plugins first |
| 3.6i | Check sets: presets per platform or role (e.g. "web server", "database host") that create several check definitions at once | idea | Split from 3.6b on 2026-11-13. The seed set (cpu, memory, disk, service, network, uptime) is the only preset today. |
| 3.6j | Arguments from the manager for script checks: per-command opt-in in `agent.yaml` with validated placeholders | done | **Done 2026-11-26** (branch `feat/3.6j-script-check-arguments`). Plan: `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-26-script-check-arguments-3.6j.md`. A command in `agent.yaml` may be a mapping: `run` (the command line; an entry that is exactly `{name}` is a placeholder, placeholders are whole arguments only, never the program) and `args` (per placeholder exactly one rule: `pattern` (RE2, anchored by the agent, at most 512 bytes, `allow_leading_dash` to admit a leading `-`), `min` and `max`, or `values`; optional `default`). A placeholder without a rule, a rule without a placeholder, an embedded placeholder or a bad rule refuses the file at load; the list form keeps its literal 3.6g meaning. The `script` check gains `args`, a list of `command:placeholder=value` (shared rules in the leaf `internal/scriptargs`; the manager checks the shape only: listed command, placeholder once, at most 16 values per command, values 1 to 512 bytes without control characters). The agent binds the values through `scriptargs.Template.Bind` before the file checks and the run: shape again, then the host's rule; a value for a command without placeholders, an undeclared placeholder, a missing value without default, a leading `-` the rule does not allow, and cmd.exe metacharacters and delimiters (`" % ^ & | < > ! ( ) , ; =`) for a batch program are refused as status unknown naming the placeholder, never the value. Security review fix round 1: values are printable ASCII unless the rule sets `unicode: true` (Windows best-fit), format characters, separators and non-characters are refused everywhere, a Windows program's value starting with `/` needs `allow_leading_dash`, a `..` path segment needs `allow_dotdot: true`, a bound value the file check refuses is reported by a generic text, and a Windows program path naming a stream is refused at load. Values are separate argv elements, no shell, no environment from the manager; a bound absolute path passes the 3.6g file check. No proto, heartbeat or migration change: an older agent refuses the mapping form (whole file, as with `commands:`), and runs a list-form command unchanged if a check sends values (a new agent refuses them). Left out: `--opt={x}` templates, a per-placeholder opt-out of the file check, reporting command names and rules in the heartbeat for early validation in the GUI. |
| 3.6c | Discovery of items (mounts, services, interfaces, containers) with per-item overrides | done | v0.3b-3 (`internal/items`, migration 00048): discovered items live in `check_items` — one row per (host, check_key, item) the evaluation ticker's own `Load` inserts on every tick (`ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`, `first_seen` never moves), covering mounts, service units and anything else a plugin reports with a non-empty item name; a row is never deleted, so a mount gone for two weeks and back is still the same item. An operator can decide one item — or a `*` pattern of them — should not be judged: a decision (`check_item_decisions`) binds at the organization or a single host, names an exact item or a glob, says `ignore` or `watch`, and a host decision always outranks an organization one (`host` beats `org`, then `exact` beats `glob`, then the older `created_at` — the same strict total order `rules.Resolve` already uses). Ignoring drops the tuple's stored state so a `crit` does not sit unrefreshed, but never touches what the agent is asked to measure — the reading keeps arriving, only the verdict stops. The Items tab (`web/src/features/checks/ItemsPage.tsx`) lists every decision with a pattern editor that previews its match count before anything is saved; the host page's own monitoring card offers a one-click ignore/watch per row. `internal/e2e/items_test.go` and `web/e2e/items.spec.ts` prove the arc end to end: a mount crossing a threshold reaches `crit`, ignoring it returns the host to `ok` with the tuple carrying no state, watching it again returns it to `crit`. **Half of this row's own text had already shipped in v0.3b-2, unmarked at the time**: a rule has bound to `(host, check_key, item)` since 3.6d (`rules.ScopeItem`), item already beat host beat group beat organization there too, and "new items inherit rules" — this row's own original note — was never a feature v0.3b-3 needed to build: `rules.Resolve` judges a tuple by its `(check, item)` regardless of whether anything had "discovered" that item yet, so an item nobody had decided anything about was always implicitly watched under whatever rule already governed it (the design spec's own "What is already built" section says so in as many words). What this row's title actually named and v0.3b-3 is what built it: a record of which items exist at all, and the operator's ability to say one should *not* be judged |
| 3.6d | Rule engine: metric, operator, value, duration → state; inheritance plugin → org → group → host → item | done | v0.3b-2: `internal/rules`' AST (`ast.go`) and metric catalogue (`catalog.go`, eleven entries — see the design doc's As built section for the three `cpu.load*` ones the original spec table omitted), a recursive-descent parser (`parse.go`) and a pure evaluator (`evaluate.go`, `states.go`) turn one host's measured tuples and its effective rules into a state per tuple (`ok`/`warn`/`crit`/`unknown`) and a per-host roll-up. Precedence resolves per (tuple, severity) independently, narrowest scope wins (item > host > group > org, `resolve.go`), and the effective view (`GET /hosts/{id}/effective-rules`) names every rule a winner shadowed. A 15 s ticker (`ticker.go`) claims dirty-or-due hosts `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`, so several managers stay safe with no claim protocol of their own |
| 3.6e | Rule editor: form mode + expression mode (same rule model) | done | v0.3b-2: `web/src/features/checks/RuleDialog.tsx`, `RuleForm.tsx` and `RuleExpression.tsx`. A form-mode threshold compiles through `rules.Form.Compile`; an expression compiles through `rules.Parse`; both produce the same `Node` tree the evaluator reads, so neither editor is a second implementation of the rule language. Switching form → expression is one-way, except that the button back to form mode stays offered as long as the edited expression still fits the form's own subset (`AsForm`). A seeded rule's metric, scope and severity are fixed (copy it to change them); its threshold, clear condition, duration and enabled flag are not. `POST /rules/validate` — a route beyond the plan's own table, see the design doc's As built section — backs the editor's live validation without persisting anything, proven by a test that a validate call leaves the rule list untouched |
| 3.6f | Hysteresis / flap protection, unknown state on missing data | done | v0.3b-2: hysteresis is a rule's own clear condition (`Rule.Clear`) — a tuple standing at warn or crit holds that state until the clear predicate is true, not merely until the trigger predicate is false, and only against an improvement, never against an escalation (`evaluate.go`'s `decide`, step 3). Flapping is a per-tuple flag set at five state changes inside a rolling 30-minute window and cleared below three, counting the change the current evaluation is about to write so a tuple's fifth change flags on the very tick that produced it. `unknown` is reached three of a check's own resolved intervals after its last reading (`staleAt`), outranks nothing but a genuinely judged warn or crit (`unknown` ranks below `warn` on purpose — a known problem elsewhere on the host must not be masked by one stale mount), and is asserted through a real agent falling silent by `internal/e2e/rules_test.go` |
| 3.6g | Custom script checks: Nagios plugin format (exit code + perfdata) and JSON format | done | **Done 2026-11-14** (branch `feat/3.6g-script-checks`). Spec: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-14-script-checks-design.md`. The agent plugin `script` (`internal/agent/checks/plugins/script`; descriptor `internal/checkplugins/script.go`) runs commands named in `agent.yaml` under a new `commands:` key (name to argv, first entry an absolute path; same file, permission rule and reload as `secrets:`; the manager only ever sends names, so it cannot change what runs). Output is read as Nagios (exit code plus perfdata, units scaled to seconds/bytes) or JSON (`status`, `message`, `metrics`); readings `script.status`, `script.output`, `script.exit_code`, `script.duration_seconds`, `script.value` (item `<command>:<label>`). Safety: no shell, a built environment without the agent's token or secrets, a path check before every run of the program and of every absolute-path argument (the agent walks the path name by name, following links itself, and every directory a name is looked up in, where links lie and where they lead included, must be root-owned and not writable by others, on Windows owned by an administrator principal with no swap rights for others, as must the file, a directory the path names and the directory a final link lies in; a missing name passes only in such a directory; FUSE file systems on the way are refused; the command then runs exactly as configured; Unix and Windows variants), a timeout that kills the whole process tree, 64 KiB output cap, 64 values per command, at most four commands of a check at once. Migration 00094 seeds two default rules, "Script reports critical" and "Script reports warning". User docs: Script checks and Agent configuration in noix-docs. Manager-supplied arguments: 3.6j |
| 3.6h | HTTP check with JSONPath extraction, SNMP OID checks | done | **Done 2026-11-15** (branch `feat/3.6h-http-json-values`; SNMP half by 3.13d). Spec: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-15-http-json-values-design.md`. The `http` probe gains `json_values` (`name=JSONPath`, at most 32, GET only): a path must select one scalar, a number becomes `http.json.value`, a string or boolean `http.json.text`, anything else is an error on that item only. Paths are a bounded subset of RFC 9535 parsed and evaluated by `internal/jpath` (no unions, slices, functions, nested filters or `$` in filters; one `..`; filters at most 8 comparisons and 32 nodes), bounded by a node-visit budget (2 M per path, 8 M per check), a nesting limit of 64, 64-character numbers and a process-wide JSON stage gate of `min(2, GOMAXPROCS)` slots. Optional `secret` (organization secret store): `username`+`password`, `token` or `header`+`value`, never followed to another scheme, host or port. No migration, no new seed rules. User docs: HTTP checks (new page, `reference/http-checks.md`), Check plugins, Organization secrets and Changelog in noix-docs. Follow-up: 3.71 |
| 3.7 | System checks: CPU, load, memory, swap, disk usage, inodes, network, uptime | done | **Done 2026-11-11.** The agent gained a `network` plugin (per interface `net.rx_bytes_per_s`, `net.tx_bytes_per_s`, `net.rx_errors_per_s`, `net.tx_errors_per_s`, `net.rx_drops_per_s`, `net.tx_drops_per_s`, rates from counter deltas; every check key keeps its own baselines, so a rate is always over that key's own interval however long; the first collection, a counter reset and an interface back from being down or gone report nothing and re-baseline; virtual devices are excluded by name prefix unless listed explicitly) and an `uptime` plugin (`uptime.seconds`); both are in the rules catalogue, so a rule can name them (`uptime.seconds < 600` catches a restart). `SeedDefinitions()` seeds `network` (60 s) and `uptime` (5 min) org-bound for new organizations, and migration 00084 backfills existing ones. A key an operator already uses at the organization scope, or that a shared check definition holds, is not seeded (the shared definition keeps it). Earlier text: v0.3b-1 ships six of the eight as agent plugins over gopsutil: `cpu.load1/5/15` + `cpu.used_pct`, `mem.used_pct` + `mem.available_bytes`, `swap.used_pct`, and per mount `disk.used_pct` + `disk.free_bytes` + `disk.inodes_used_pct` (an optional mount list, otherwise every real filesystem). Network and uptime have no plugin |
| 3.8 | Service checks: systemd / Windows services / launchd | done | **Done 2026-11-12, spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-12-windows-macos-agents-design.md`.** The `service` plugin reads systemd units on Linux, service names (not display names) on Windows and launchd labels of the system domain on macOS; the state tables are in the spec. The seeded `sshd` check reads unknown on macOS and on Windows without OpenSSH server, raises no alert, and is overridden with a group for `os:darwin` / `os:windows`. Earlier: v0.3b-1 shipped the plugin for systemd |
| 3.9 | Hardware: SMART, temperatures, RAID (mdadm) | done | **Done 2026-11-12, spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-12-hardware-checks-design.md`.** Three agent plugins, none seeded: `temperature` (`temp.celsius`, `temp.high_celsius`, `temp.critical_celsius` per OS sensor; Linux hwmon/thermal, macOS, Windows ACPI thermal zones; duplicate names get `#2`, `#3`; `temp.sensors` count on item `""`), `smart` (smartctl from smartmontools, looked up in the standard install locations before `PATH`, on Windows only under `%ProgramFiles%`; `smart.passed`, temperature, power-on hours, reallocated/pending/offline-uncorrectable sectors, NVMe wear and error counters; `smart.devices` count on item `""`, which also carries the host-level errors including "no device with SMART data" when at least one device answers without SMART, no device has SMART data and none sleeps (a round where every device failed shows the count; 3.55); sleeping disks are not woken and read unknown while asleep (3.9b); disks behind RAID controllers are named `<device> [<type>]`; `devices` entries must start with `/dev/` and have no `..` segment) and `raid` (Linux mdadm only: `raid.state`, device counts, `raid.sync_pct` on every run (100 when no sync runs), `raid.arrays`; Intel RST/VROC and DDF containers skipped). The host-level counts exist so that an error on item `""` is replaced by the next good run, and every metric is sent on every run so that no value goes stale by design. Params are validated in `internal/checks/hardware_params.go`; the three lists bound the items like `mounts` (3.28), with item `""` always asked. The SPA offers the three plugins in the check form. Older agents skip the plugins (see `docs/upgrades.md`). **Real-hardware validation is still pending:** the plugins were built against recorded smartctl, sensor and `/proc/mdstat` outputs and have not run on real disks yet. To validate: SATA and NVMe disks, a failing disk, a disk that spins down, USB bridges with and without SMART pass-through, card readers and USB slots without media, a SAS disk in standby (whether smartctl prints no `smart_support` block), a RAID controller (megaraid), a virtual machine, macOS and Windows sensors and smartctl, and md arrays through a resync, recovery and check. End-user docs: `reference/check-plugins.md` and `user/monitoring.md` in noix-docs. ZFS is 3.9a |
| 3.9a | ZFS pool health | done | **Done 2026-11-15** (branch `feat/3.9a-zfs-pools`). Spec: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-15-zfs-pools-design.md`. Agent plugin `zfs` (Linux only, none seeded), built like `raid`: one item per pool plus the host item `""` (`zfs.pools`). Metrics `zfs.state`, `zfs.capacity_pct`, `zfs.size_bytes`, `zfs.free_bytes`, `zfs.fragmentation_pct`, `zfs.errors_read`/`_write`/`_checksum`, `zfs.data_errors`, `zfs.devices_unhealthy`, `zfs.scan`, `zfs.scan_pct`, `zfs.scrub_age_days`. Data from the text output of `zpool list -Hp`, `zpool status -p` and (only when needed) `zfs get creation`, looked up in the standard locations before `PATH`; no `/dev/zfs` or no `zpool` means 0 pools. Several `scan:` entries per pool are read (sequential rebuild, dRAID, 2.2 error scrub). Scrub age: finished scrub printed now (remembered per pool in memory) > start of a running or paused scrub > remembered end > pool creation; a canceled scrub, a resilver and an error scrub are not finished scrubs; the memory is lost on agent restart. No migration. Older agents skip the plugin (see `docs/upgrades.md`). **Not validated on real hardware** (3.72). End-user docs: `reference/check-plugins.md`, `user/monitoring.md` and the changelog in noix-docs |
| 3.9b | smart: opt-in waking or standby-aware state for disks that spin down | done | Done 2026-11-25 (`fix/4.42-3.9b-fresh-since-smart-standby`, owner decision: standby-aware state): the smart check reports `smart.power_mode` (`standby`/`active`); descriptors declare `HeldWhile` (smart standby; zfs EXPORTED/MISSING from 3.74, which moved from not-asked to held), and a held tuple keeps its state, alert and place in the roll-up while the judging rule still applies and no newer reading exists; the item expiry skips held tuples; on wake the first reading decides and a for-duration wait restarts. Originally: Since 3.9 the `smart` check leaves a sleeping disk asleep (`-n standby,0`) and reports nothing for it, so its values turn stale and read unknown — and the host unknown, with a host-scope alert — while it sleeps; after the item-expiry period (seven days by default, 3.12b) a disk that never wakes is forgotten together with its open alerts. Until then the docs recommend listing only always-on disks in `devices` or ignoring spin-down disks. Options: a per-check opt-in to wake disks, or a standby-aware state (the agent reports that the disk sleeps, and its last values do not turn unknown meanwhile); split from 3.9 |
| 3.10 | Database checks: PostgreSQL, MySQL/MariaDB, Redis | done | **Done 2026-11-12, spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-12-database-checks-design.md` (branch `feat/3.10-database-checks`).** Three agent plugins, `postgres` (PostgreSQL 10+), `mysql` (MySQL 8.0+, MariaDB 10.5+) and `redis` (Redis 5+, Valkey), each reading a server the host can reach with the credentials of an `agent.yaml` secret (backlog 3.5; required for `postgres` and `mysql`, optional for `redis`, where none means `127.0.0.1:6379` without login). Metrics: `pg.*` (up, connections, max and percentage, in_recovery, replication lag in seconds and bytes, oldest client transaction, per-database size), `mysql.*` (up, connections, max and percentage, replication state and lag across every channel or named connection, slow queries per second), `redis.*` (up, memory used, max and percentage, clients, role, replication link and lag, evicted keys per second). Error texts are fixed classes, never a server's message. The four safety preconditions from the 3.5 final review are done: (1) a panic in a `SecretPlugin` is recovered by `internal/agent/checks/secret.go` (`c06a62b3`), (2) the registry cross-check in `internal/checkplugins/crosscheck_test.go` (every secret plugin names a non-empty `SecretErrorMetric` that its descriptor lists), (3) the positive `missing_secret` test through `internal/api/apitest` (`TestHostChecksCarryTheMissingSecretOfAPostgresCheck`), (4) the `secret` parameter hint in the check forms (`checks.params.*.secret`). An older agent skips these checks with a warning (`docs/upgrades.md`). Licence note: `go-sql-driver/mysql` is MPL-2.0, the first MPL dependency in the agent (compatible when distributed unmodified). Follow-ups: 3.10a to 3.10c |
| 3.10a | Client certificates and custom CAs for the `mysql` and `redis` checks; Windows named pipes | done | **Done 2026-11-26** (branch `feat/3.10a-db-tls-client-certs`, plan `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-26-db-tls-client-certs-3.10a.md`). New `agent.yaml` secret fields: `mysql` and `redis` take `tls_ca` and `tls_server_name` (only with `tls: true`) and `tls_cert`/`tls_key` (a pair, any TLS mode); `postgres` takes `sslcert`/`sslkey` (libpq's names, not with `sslmode: disable`). Every file field is an absolute path read on each round through the shared `dbcheck.TLSFiles`: a regular PEM file of at most 1 MiB, the key unencrypted (encrypted keys are refused with a clear text), matching the certificate and, on Unix, not accessible to other users (group read allowed). Each file is opened once (non-blocking, so a FIFO cannot hang a round) and checked on the open descriptor; `mysql`/`redis` accept key files that start with `EC PARAMETERS` (`openssl ecparam -genkey`), `postgres` (whose driver reads only the first block) names that case. `postgres` refuses `sslcert`/`sslkey` with a socket-directory `host`, where no TLS is used. Errors name the field and a fixed reason, never the path. `mysql` now hands the driver a `tls.Config` instead of a registry name. `mysql` also takes a Windows-only `pipe` (a name or `\\.\pipe\<name>`, local only, dialed through go-winio; other systems refuse it; the docs recommend `tls: true` with `tls_ca` for it, since any local user can create a pipe under the server's name while it is down). A TLS alert sent by the server now reads "TLS handshake failed" (except `mysql`, whose driver hides a client certificate the server rejects after the handshake as "connection failed"). `sslrootcert` must now be absolute (`docs/upgrades.md`). Real-server tests for all three plugins with a throwaway CA and a server that requires a client certificate. Left out: named pipes for `redis` (no supported Redis listens on one on Windows) and `postgres` (PostgreSQL has no pipe listener); key passphrases. Was: `postgres` can verify against a CA file (`sslrootcert`), `mysql` and `redis` only use the system roots (`tls=true`) or skip verification (`skip-verify`), and neither offers a client certificate. Unix sockets work on Linux and macOS only; a Windows server listening on a named pipe is unreachable. Split from 3.10 |
| 3.10b | More database metrics: Redis persistence status, PostgreSQL per-replica lag on the primary, MySQL per-schema sizes | done | **Done 2026-11-26** (branch `feat/3.10b-database-metrics`, plan `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-26-database-metrics-3.10b.md`). `postgres` reads `pg_stat_replication` on every server: `pg.replicas` on item "" and, per replica item `replica:<application_name>@<client host>` (`local` over a Unix socket, the host alone without a name), `pg.replica_lag_bytes` (from this server's WAL position to the replica's replay position), `pg.replica_lag_seconds` (0 while nothing is behind, else the first of replay, flush and write lag; left out of a round while bytes are behind and no lag time is measured yet) and `pg.replica_state`; only senders that replay count (pg_basebackup, pg_receivewal, barman_receive_wal and logical table-sync workers are left out, and a standby counts from its first reply with a replay position), senders sharing a key report the one furthest behind, at most 64 replica items a round; without `pg_read_all_stats`, or when the replica read fails, only `pg.replicas` carries the error and the round stays up. A retired replica, or one that changes address, leaves a stale item to forget. The descriptor asks for replica items whatever `databases` lists (`ItemAsked`). `redis` reads the persistence fields of the INFO it already fetches (no new command or ACL): `redis.rdb_last_bgsave_status`, `redis.aof_last_write_status`, `redis.aof_last_bgrewrite_status` (ok/err), `redis.rdb_last_save_age_seconds` (on the server's clock where INFO gives `server_time_usec`), `redis.rdb_changes_since_last_save`, `redis.aof_enabled`, `redis.loading`. `mysql` takes the optional `schema_sizes` (default false, because `information_schema.TABLES` opens every table on MariaDB): `mysql.schema_size_bytes` per schema (data + index, a schema without tables 0, system schemas left out, at most 256; only schemas the login holds a privilege on) and `mysql.schemas_unreported`, which also carries the error of a failed sizes query while the round stays up; the query runs under a server-side bound a tenth below the client's 5 s deadline (MariaDB `SET STATEMENT max_statement_time … FOR`, else MySQL's `MAX_EXECUTION_TIME` hint after a syntax error, like the replication chain), so the server stops it when the client gives up; both are asked only while the parameter is on. `SHOW VIEW` is enough to see sizes without reading data (pinned by the integration test on MySQL 8.4 and MariaDB 11.4). **Left out:** a `schemas` filter list for mysql; detecting a Redis server without snapshots configured (its save age simply grows; `CONFIG GET` needs more than `+info`); fresher MySQL 8 sizes than its statistics cache (`information_schema_stats_expiry`, 24 h by default) gives |
| 3.10c | Advisory errors are dropped by the scheduler, so names a plugin left out are visible to nobody | done | **Done 2026-11-13** (branch `fix/3.x-correctness`). Two new count metrics on item `""`, reported on every round (also failed ones): `pg.databases_unreported` (databases over the 256 cap, exact through a window count, plus refused names) and `docker.containers_unreported` (refused names). The scheduler also logs a plugin's advisory error on the agent, once per change and again after an hour, with check, plugin and advisory only; a secret-reading plugin's advisory is dropped. Was: `postgres` reports at most 256 database sizes and names the rest (and any name the item contract refuses) in an advisory error; the scheduler drops advisory errors, so with more than 256 databases the remainder is silently unreported. The same holds for the `docker` plugin's refused container names (3.12). Consider surfacing them, for example as an error observation on a plugin-level metric or a line in the host's check status. Split from 3.10 |
| 3.11 | Web server checks: nginx, Apache, Caddy status endpoints, PHP-FPM | done | **Done 2026-11-13, spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-13-web-server-checks-design.md` (branch `feat/3.11-web-server-checks`).** Four agent plugins reading the status page of a server the host can reach (URL as a parameter, a localhost default each): `nginx` (`stub_status`; `nginx.*` up, connections by state, requests, accepted and dropped connections per second), `apache` (`mod_status` `?auto`; `apache.*` up, busy and idle workers, busy percentage, requests and bytes per second), `caddy` (metrics endpoint, Prometheus text format; `caddy.*` up, requests in flight, requests and 5xx per second, counted per handler with the largest handler per server) and `phpfpm` (pool status `?json`; `phpfpm.*` up, processes active, idle, total and busy percentage, listen queue, requests, max-children-reached and slow requests per second). The `phpfpm` items are the URL entries (one pool each, at most 16) plus `phpfpm.pools_down` on item `""`, which also carries a missing secret. An optional secret supplies HTTP basic authentication through the fields `basic_auth_user` and `basic_auth_password`; the names are distinct from every database plugin's on purpose, because unknown fields are refused and a database secret (host, user, password) must not be sent as basic authentication to whatever URL a check names. No redirects are followed and no proxy is used, so credentials stay on the connection the check names. Rates are absent in the first round and after a counter reset. One shared helper reads at most 4 MiB per page, `phpfpm` at most 1 MiB per pool; an error text never carries the URL, a header or the body. An older agent skips these checks with a warning (`docs/upgrades.md`). Follow-ups: 3.11a to 3.11c |
| 3.11a | PHP-FPM over FastCGI directly | done | **Done 2026-11-26, plan `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-26-phpfpm-fastcgi-3.11a.md` (branch `feat/3.11a-phpfpm-fastcgi`).** A `phpfpm` `urls` entry may now be the pool's own FastCGI address with its status path, `fcgi://host:port/path` (an IP literal, IPv6 in brackets, or a DNS name, and a port) or `unix:/socket:/path` (an absolute socket path of at most 103 bytes, macOS's limit), next to the status page URLs, mixed freely; each entry stays one pool and its item. The grammar lives once in `internal/checkplugins` (format `phpfpmPool`, with its TypeScript twin and shared vectors), used by the manager, the agent's local checks and the plugin. The agent talks FastCGI itself through a minimal in-repo client (`plugins/internal/fastcgi`: one responder request per connection, a fixed set of params whose only variable values are the path and `json`, the path only in SCRIPT_NAME / DOCUMENT_URI / REQUEST_URI and SCRIPT_FILENAME always `/..`, the body bounded at 1 MiB, the header block at 16 KiB, STDERR at 64 KiB and the raw stream in all, the 5 s query timeout); the CGI `Status` maps to the same texts as HTTP, and a missing or forbidden socket reads "socket not found" / "permission denied". FPM recognises its status page by the request URI from SCRIPT_NAME and refuses a `..` path segment before its extension check and its doc_root/user_dir lookup, so a request that is not the status page is never executed and answers 404 (403 under some pool settings). Pinned against real PHP-FPM 8.3 in the container test with three pools — the default, one with `security.limit_extensions` emptied, and one with that plus `cgi.fix_pathinfo=0` and `doc_root` — and a dotless PHP file present: the status page is read from each over TCP, no other path runs the file, and every such path reads the same 404. The status path is also `/` and letters, digits, `_ / -` only — no dot (never a `.php` file) and no `~` (no `/~user/` lookup) — as defence in depth. The basic-auth secret and `verify_tls` apply to URL entries only. An older agent reads a FastCGI entry as a URL its HTTP client refuses, so the pool reads down with "connection failed" until it is updated (`docs/upgrades.md`). Left out: status paths with a dot or a query (`full`), a default port or path, abstract unix sockets, and a real-FPM test over a unix socket (the socket form is tested against the in-process fake server). |
| 3.11b | More web server data: nginx Plus API, Apache `MaxRequestWorkers` and per-vhost data, Caddy upstream health | done | **Done 2026-11-26, plan `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-26-web-server-data-3.11b.md` (branch `feat/3.11b-web-server-data`).** `apache` reports `apache.workers_max` and `apache.workers_busy_max_pct` (busy workers against the limit): the `?auto` page's `Scoreboard:` line has exactly the server's effective `MaxRequestWorkers` slots — measured against Apache 2.4.38 and 2.4.71 for event (defaults 400; `MaxRequestWorkers 150`, also with `ServerLimit 32`; 160 rounded by Apache to 150), worker and prefork, and pinned by the container test — so no operator parameter is needed (the 3.11 spec's "ServerLimit × ThreadLimit" was wrong) — in normal running only: during and after a graceful restart (notably one lowering the limit) the scoreboard can keep the old, higher length until the processes then started are recycled, so the percentage reads low, never above 100 (review M1, documented, no override parameter); `apache.workers_busy_pct` keeps its meaning; a page without a scoreboard still reads, the two metrics then carry the reason. `nginx` takes an optional `api_url` (the nginx Plus API base with its version) and then also reads `/http/server_zones` and `/http/upstreams` in the same round: items `zone:<name>` (processing; request, 4xx, 5xx, received and sent byte rates) and `upstream:<name>` (peers, peers up, peers unavailable = `unavail` or `unhealthy`, active connections; request, 5xx and fail rates of the peer sums, restarted when the peer set changes), at most 64 of each by name, `nginx.api_up` and `nginx.api_items_unreported` on item `""`. `caddy` takes an optional `upstreams_url` (the admin API's `reverse_proxy/upstreams`) and then reports items `upstream:<address>` (requests in flight, counted failures, health), at most 64, with `caddy.admin_up`, `caddy.upstreams_unhealthy` and `caddy.upstreams_unreported` on item `""`;
