Target: one descriptor per plugin (name, where it runs, parameters with their validation, metrics with their kind, the parameter that enumerates items) from which the catalogue, the API enums and the item logic derive, served to the SPA so the check form renders fields and validation from it. Do it before 3.10 and 3.11, which add plugins. **Done 2026-11-12** (branch `refactor/3.54-plugin-descriptors`, spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-12-plugin-descriptors-design.md`). One descriptor per plugin in the new leaf package `internal/checkplugins`; `internal/checks` (validation, `itemParam`), `internal/rules` (catalogue) and the API plugin enums derive from it, and the new route `GET /api/v1/check-plugins` serves it so the check form is built from it (i18n keyed by plugin and param). Parity is pinned by `testdata/formats.json` (shared with the TS format twins), the SPA fixture `web/src/test/checkPlugins.json` (`-update-spa-fixture`) and the golden catalogue table in `internal/rules/catalog_test.go`; a probe pin test checks that `probe.Run` knows exactly the server plugins. Adding a plugin is now the note in `internal/agent/checks/plugins/registry.go`. **Deferred minors:** the host checks page waits for the descriptors before it renders; `ParamFields` uses a fixed `maxLength` of 500; list placeholders live in i18n, not in the descriptors; `Format` is a plain string in the OpenAPI schema. |
| 3.55 | Tuples nobody asks for are still judged, and a few hardware metrics can still go stale on their own | done | **Done 2026-11-13.** (1) The evaluation leaves an item a check no longer asks for out (interval 0 through `CheckContext.Asks`, item `""` always judged), so its open alerts close as `stale`; the effective rules view still lists them (3.56). (2) `temperature` and `smart` keep a per-item memory (`VanishedMetrics`) and report `<metric> is no longer reported` as an error for a metric that vanishes or changes type while its item still reports, for at most 24 h. (3) `smart.devices` says "no device with SMART data" only when a device answered without data and none had data or sleeps. (4) The SAS standby case is on the 3.9 validation list. Was: From the 3.9 final re-review (2026-11-12). (1) `AsksForItem`/`stillAsked` only hide an item on the host page; the rules evaluator (`internal/rules/load_sql.go`) still judges its tuples, so taking a mount, interface, unit, sensor, disk or array off a check's list hides it while it turns unknown and keeps the host unknown with a host alert whose cause is not shown, until the item expiry (3.12b). Treat a not-asked tuple like one with no resolved interval: out of evaluation, open alerts closed. (2) Conditional metrics that can strand while their item keeps reporting: `temp.high_celsius`/`temp.critical_celsius` (a limit that stops being stated) and the per-field `smart.*` metrics (a field that disappears or changes type) — the 3.9 note's "every metric is sent on every run" holds for `raid.*` and the host counts, not for these; emit an error observation for a metric that was reported before, or document it. (3) `smart.devices` says "no device with SMART data" also in a round where every device failed (the failures are on the items already); raise it only when at least one device answered without data, and align the user docs. (4) Add "SAS disk in standby" to the 3.9 real-hardware validation list: whether smartctl prints no `smart_support` block for a sleeping SAS disk is unverified |
| 3.56 | The effective rules view lists rules for items a check no longer asks for | done | `internal/api/rule_handlers.go` `toTuples` filters by check key only, so the effective-rules view (since 3.28) still lists rules for a mount, interface or unit that a check's item list no longer asks for, although the evaluation leaves it out since 3.55. Filter with the same predicate (`checkplugins.AsksForItem`, item `""` always asked) and keep the host page's `stillAsked` consistent with it. Found in the 3.55 review (Done 2026-11-18: `toTuples` now drops items `checkplugins.AsksForItem` says the check no longer asks for, item "" always kept like the evaluation; the host page's `stillAsked` already uses the same predicate, so no SPA change) |
| 3.57 | Client hosts: server or client kind per host | done | **Done 2026-11-13.** Spec: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-13-client-hosts-design.md`. A host is a server (default, all existing hosts) or a client (notebook, desktop: off by design). Migration 00090 adds `hosts.kind`, 00091 the table `org_monitoring_settings` (`client_absent_days`, default 14, 0 off, 0–365; Settings → General → Monitoring, viewer reads, admin writes), 00092 `host_agents.online_since`. A client whose agent is offline (no contact for 90 s) is frozen: no state change, no unknown host alert, an open unknown host alert closes as `stale`, tuple alerts stay; the SPA shows an Off badge with the last state and "last seen". A back client is judged from the start of its current online period (`online_since`, reset after 90 s without contact), so a 1 h check is not unknown on boot; a client shutting down gets a 90 s grace before an unknown host alert. The absent warning is a host alert with severity `warn`, "not seen since <time>", opened at the tick when the last contact plus the setting passes and closed when the client is back (a server switched to client past the threshold may open it at once). Forget is hidden for an off client. A client without an agent behaves like a server. Known gap: the fleet totals and the state filter still count an off client by its frozen roll-up, so they can say "unknown 3" while those rows read Off. Limitation: see 3.58. |
| 3.58 | The online window ignores an agent's own heartbeat interval | done | Done 2026-11-24 on `fix/small-items-3.58-3.67-3.71` (`e4c76b33`): the agent reports its heartbeat interval in `Facts.heartbeat_interval_seconds` (first message after every reconnect), stored in `host_agents.heartbeat_interval_s` (migration 00119; 5 s–10 min, else NULL); `agents.OnlineWindow` = max(3 × interval, 90 s), 90 s when unknown, replaces `StaleAfter` everywhere — the agents list, `online_since` in Touch and Heartbeat (3.118's replay hold), the rule evaluation's presence and unknown grace. Left: the first touch after an agent restarts with a different interval uses the stored one until its facts arrive; the online period restarts after a gap longer than the window or the agent's 15 min disconnect buffer, whichever is shorter (`51a1caf3`), so 3.118's replay hold arms for slow agents too; the manager's bounds and buffer window are tied to the agent's by `TestManagerMirrorsTheAgentsBounds`, but `rules.replayHoldCap` (also 15 min) is not tied to `agents.DisconnectBufferWindow` by any test |
| 3.59 | Select hosts by kind in a monitoring-group selector | idea | From 3.57 (non-goal there). Tags already do it (tag clients `role=client`); a selector term on `kind` would make it explicit. |
| 3.60 | Installer re-run without a token | done | Done 2026-11-24 on `fix/small-items-3.60-3.68-3.4b` (`de6cc844`, `1be43b63`, `927a674f`): both installers reuse an identity found in the data folder (`agent.crt`, `agent.key`, `agent.ca.pem`, each a non-empty regular file) without asking for a token, write the env file without `PULSIX_ENROLL_TOKEN` and say so; an explicit token is written as before. The three CI install smokes plant an identity and upgrade without a token. Not run locally (no PowerShell here, installers never run on the dev Mac): the first CI run is the first execution of the Windows paths. Originally: an upgrade over an existing identity (or a reinstall after `--keep-data`) still asks for an enrollment token and stops if the answer is empty, although the agent never uses the token while its identity is valid. The installer should skip the prompt when a valid identity exists in the data folder (found 2026-11-14 in the 3.49/3.51 review) |

Select only the identity columns Done 2026-11-18: LookupAgentSerial and LockHostAgentsByName select only the identity columns; query-text guard test in internal/agents. |
| 3.62 | Dedupe the manager's per-heartbeat warning for a dropped local check | done | From 3.5a. While an agent reports a local check the manager refuses (for example a plugin the manager does not know), the manager logs a warning on every heartbeat. Log once per change of the dropped set per agent Done 2026-11-18: the warning is logged once per change of an agent's dropped set (in-memory, per service, forgotten when the set empties; the map is keyed by certificate serial and not bounded, so the entry of a renewed or revoked agent stays until restart). |
| 3.63 | Show the local checks the manager drops under the host's local check problems | done | From the 3.5a final review (finding 4). A check the agent runs but the manager refuses (in practice version skew: a newer agent with a plugin or parameter this manager does not know) runs on the host and its values are stored, but the host page neither shows nor judges it and nothing appears under "Problems with local checks": only the manager's log has it (3.62). Append one bounded problem per dropped check to the stored problems, within the 32 cap, with the key and the manager's fixed reason (never params); update the user docs' wording of the problems list Done 2026-11-18: one problem per dropped check, `<key>: ignored by the server (<reason>)`, and `<n> more local checks: ignored by the server (more than 64 local checks reported)` for entries never examined, after the agent's own problems within the 32 cap. |

Negligible for typical files, noticeable only at the caps times many hosts. Write the two columns only when they differ (`CASE WHEN local_checks IS DISTINCT FROM $x THEN $x ELSE local_checks END`, which also keeps the old TOAST pointer) and/or cache the decoded report per host for the evaluation Done 2026-11-18: RecordAgentHeartbeat writes local_checks and local_check_problems only when they differ (IS DISTINCT FROM guards, text-guarded by a test); the per-host decode cache was not added. |

(3) `SetProbesSupported` is one UPDATE per heartbeat (a no-op when unchanged); fold it into the heartbeat UPDATE. |

| 3.84 | Other request methods and custom request headers or bodies | done | From 3.13b. The `http` check sends GET or HEAD only, with no request headers or body of its own (credentials come from a secret). Add `POST` and friends, headers and a body. **Done 2026-11-26** (branch `feat/3.84-http-methods-headers-body`, plan `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-26-http-methods-headers-body-3.84.md`). `method` takes GET, HEAD, POST, PUT, PATCH, DELETE and OPTIONS; new `headers` (at most 16 `Name: value` entries of at most 1 024 bytes, the name an RFC 9110 token, no control characters but tab, a name at most once; refused: `Host`, `Content-Length`, `Transfer-Encoding`, `Connection`, `User-Agent`, `Keep-Alive`, `Proxy-Connection`, `TE`, `Trailer`, `Upgrade`, `Expect`, `Accept-Encoding`, `Content-Type`, and the credential headers `Authorization`, `Proxy-Authorization`, `Cookie`, which belong in the check's secret), `body` (text, at most 8 KiB, only with POST, PUT or PATCH) and `content_type` (a media type, only with a body; `application/json` when a body is sent without one). The rules live in `internal/probe/httpreq`, read by the descriptor (formats `httpHeader` and `mediaType`, SPA twins on shared vectors) and the probe alike; the probe refuses params past validation without quoting them, sets the plain headers before the secret's header (which wins), drops both when a redirect leaves the first origin, and a 307/308 resends the body while a 301/302/303 turns into a GET (net/http's browser rules). No confirmation flag per check for state-changing methods: the form warns under the method. Fix round 1 (review): POST, PUT, PATCH, DELETE and a body need the manager setting `PULSIX_PROBE_WRITE_METHODS` (default off; refused on save with a message naming it, and a stored one fails its attempt with that reason instead of probing; carried by the probe guard, so an agent never sends one); routing and override headers (`X-Forwarded-*`, `Forwarded`, `X-Real-IP`, `X-Original-URL`, `X-Rewrite-URL`, method overrides) and credential-looking names (containing token, secret, apikey, api-key, api_key, password, access-key, access_key or session, or the segment auth) are refused; with the switch off a stored write stays editable while its request is unchanged; the secret's own header follows the reserved and routing name rules (a secret naming such a header fails the check); plain headers are dropped when a redirect leaves the first origin; `content_type` advertises no default; the body must be UTF-8 text without NUL. Skew: an agent of an earlier release would drop headers and a body and send a plain GET, and there is no capability bit for probe features, so a check with a custom request is probed only from the Pulsix server (new descriptor hook `ManagerOnly`, `Descriptor.ProbedOnlyFromManager`; validation refuses `vantage_groups`, the runner and the host-page view ignore them), like a check with a secret; an older manager alongside would probe such a stored check as a plain GET (`docs/upgrades.md`). SPA: request headers and body fields (body as a textarea), the method warning, the vantage group picker closed with the reason. Left out: agent vantages for custom requests (needs a heartbeat capability list, an additive column and per-check eligibility in the vantage planner), a `Host` override for a virtual host by address (needs SNI handling), a custom `User-Agent`, binary bodies, bodies with GET/DELETE/OPTIONS, secrets referenced from header values (one secret per check, as before) |

| 3.87 | Assertions on plugins other than http | done | From 3.13b. The assertion grammar and the severity per statement for `tcp`, `dns`, `tls` and `snmp`. **Done 2026-11-26** (branch `feat/3.87-assertions-other-plugins`, plan `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-26-assertions-other-plugins-3.87.md`) for `tcp`, `dns` and `tls`; `snmp` split out as 3.134. 3.13b's grammar gains dialects (`internal/probe/assert`, one subject list per plugin, `Parse` stays http's): `tcp` takes `connect_ms`, `response_ms` (dial start to the end of the response read) and `response` (the first line the target sent after `send`, without its line break; the probe then reads until that line ended and `expect` was found, within 4 KiB and the timeout); `dns` takes `resolve_ms`, `answer_count`, `answers` (sorted, joined with ", ", names lower-cased without the trailing dot, TXT as received) and `answer("x")` (`exists`/`!exists` only, compared like `expect`); `tls` takes `handshake_ms`, `cert_valid`, `cert_days`, `cert_issuer`, `cert_subject`, `cert_sans`, `version` (1.0 to 1.3) and `cipher`. A numeric subject without a value (`response_ms` when nothing arrived) fails every operator seeing `absent`. Results as for http: `<plugin>.assertion` (`pass`/`warn`/`crit`) and `<plugin>.assertion_actual` per entry item, only on an up attempt, errors on both for an attempt that could not be made; shared evaluation in `internal/probe/judge` (http keeps its own and shares the actual formatting). Descriptors: parameter `assertions` (formats `tcpAssertion`, `dnsAssertion`, `tlsAssertion` with SPA twins), `ItemParam` `assertions` with the whole-host item always asked; the three plugins gain their own `FailAll` (an unattempted `tcp` check without `expect` no longer errors `tcp.banner_match`). Migration 00134 and `rules.SeedRules()` seed `TCP/DNS/TLS assertion failed` (warn) and `(critical)`; the alert detail reads any plugin's assertion in its dialect. Skew: an entry an up vantage round left out (an older agent ignores the parameter) records the error "the vantage point that answered did not evaluate this assertion; update its agent" (`judge.Unevaluated`, gated in the probe runner's combining: only after the same vantage left entries out of two distinct rounds stamped after the check's assertions last changed plus the push horizon (an agent's longest online window without a heartbeat, 3 × the 10 min maximum heartbeat, + first-run window 1 min + 1 min slack, about 32 min; reset on a manager start or lock takeover), so an edit, a partly stored round or an outage replay never flags a current agent, and an older agent is flagged after about half an hour; http included). SPA: the assertion editor takes the plugin's subjects, the host page's assertion table (with 3.89's actions) shows for every plugin with assertions. Left out: DNS rcode (an up lookup is always NOERROR) and TTL (the system resolver does not expose it), new tls metrics for version or cipher (assertion subjects only) |
| 3.88 | Stale current-value rows when a metric stops being reported | done | From 3.13b (pre-existing, widened by it). A `check_current` row is never deleted, so a metric a changed configuration no longer reports stays at its last value: removing `body_contains` leaves `http.body_match` stale, changing a URL from a name to an IP leaves `http.dns_ms` stale. Done 2026-11-18 (spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-18-vanished-metrics-and-assertion-actions-design.md`): a metric whose reading trails its own item's newest by more than three of the check's intervals is *vanished* (`rules.Vanished`, one function for rule loading and the host page): it leaves the evaluation like an item the check no longer asks for (unmeasured, its alerts close as stale, out of the host roll-up), and `GET /hosts/{id}/checks` marks the value row `vanished: true` (additive). Plugin descriptors gain an optional `MetricAsked` hook; `http` no longer asks for `http.body_match` without `body_contains` (host page, rule loading, effective rules). The hourly expiry loop (same advisory lock and `PULSIX_ITEM_EXPIRY` period) also forgets a metric whose reading trails its item's newest by more than that period (`ListExpiredMetrics`, `checks.Service.ForgetExpiredMetrics`), in `forgetItem`'s lock order: current row, states and state changes of that metric deleted, its open alert closed as `forgotten`, host marked dirty, the item's `check_items` row kept. `DELETE /hosts/{id}/items` takes an optional `metric` query parameter that forgets just that metric (`checks.Service.ForgetMetric`, same role, 404 when nothing to forget, 400 when empty or repeated, audit summary names the metric). The SPA side landed with 3.89 (2026-11-18): a vanished row is muted with a "No longer reported" marker instead of the verdict pill and without stale or flapping markers, its Forget passes `metric` and so removes only that metric (the confirm text says so), and vanished rows no longer count in the host and check status, the key value or the State tiles. Final review (2026-11-18): only an error-free reading (a value or a label) counts as the item's newest, in `rules.ItemNewest`, `ListExpiredMetrics` and `MetricNewest` alike; an error proves the collection ran, not that the other metrics stopped, so a SMART disk smartctl cannot open (one error on `smart.passed`) keeps its other metrics judged (stale, unknown, alerts open) instead of vanishing them; an error row is judged against that newest like any other row. A disabled check never reports a value as vanished. A manual metric forget of the item's last current row is refused with `409` (`checks.ErrLastMetric`, checked under the host's evaluation lock) because it would orphan the item's `check_items` row; forget the item instead. Known and documented in `docs/upgrades.md`: the effect is not http-specific; a metric reads unknown for up to about one interval before it vanishes (on a host without an unknown grace the host `unknown` alert opens and closes); frozen clients' alerts close only when the agent is back or as forgotten; with `PULSIX_ITEM_EXPIRY` below 3 × a check's interval a metric can be forgotten before it vanishes; an older manager running alongside ignores `metric` and forgets the whole item. Follow-ups: 3.117, 3.118 |
| 3.89 | Assertion items listed as generic value rows on the host page | done | From 3.13b. Assertion items also showed as generic value rows next to the assertion table. Done 2026-11-18: the generic list leaves out an http check's `http.assertion` and `http.assertion_actual` rows (`withoutAssertionRows`), and the assertion table takes optional per-row actions and detail (an actions column only when a row has some): Ignore/Watch (item decision) and Forget (same gating as generic rows, item-wide) for a writer, a Rules toggle for the effective rules of the assertion, and the ignored marker; a viewer gets no write actions |
| 3.90 | CSS sibling combinators and more pseudo-classes for html assertions | done | From 3.13b. `+` and `~` and further pseudo-classes for `html(...)`, with a bounded matcher like the current one. Done 2026-11-26 (`feat/3.90-html-sibling-combinators`, plan `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-26-html-selector-siblings-3.90.md`): the `html(…)` subset (`internal/probe/assert`) gains the `+` (next element sibling) and `~` (any later element sibling) combinators, `:only-child`, `:nth-last-child()`, `:first-of-type`, `:last-of-type`, `:only-of-type`, `:nth-of-type()`, `:nth-last-of-type()`, `:empty` (no child nodes; whitespace text counts), `:root` (parent is the document; the probe's tree implies no `html`) and `:not(compound, …)` (no combinators, no nested `:not`; it and every simple selector inside count toward the 16 per compound); the nth forms take CSS's `An+B` with \|A\|, \|B\| ≤ 1 000 000, so `:nth-child(0)`, `(-1)` and `(+2)`, refused before, are now valid CSS (the first two select nothing). The matcher (`internal/probe/http`) stays one walk: a parent's children share a record of the states earlier siblings hand on (text never breaks adjacency), of-type positions are counted only for a selector that uses them (charged one step per element and its name's bytes); bounds (256 bytes, 8 compounds, 16 simple selectors, 15 M / 60 M steps) unchanged, hostile sibling chains, distinct sibling names and `:not` lists pinned in `TestSelectAllBoundsHostilePages`. No version gate: earlier parsers refuse every new form, so an older agent vantage reports "the assertion is not valid" rather than another verdict (`docs/upgrades.md`). SPA: the editor's selector hint (en/de); the SPA still checks only a selector's shape. Left out: `:has`, `:is`/`:where`, `:nth-child(… of S)`, user-action and form-state pseudo-classes, `:lang`/`:dir`, combinators inside `:not` |
| 3.91 | A way to find the API explorer in the GUI | done | The explorer (1.8, `/api/explorer`, behind an MFA-complete session) had no link anywhere in the SPA. The account menu in the sidebar footer now opens it in a new tab, and the API tokens page points to it under its title. Both are plain links to the page the manager serves; the server gate is unchanged |
| 3.92 | The advisory lockers can deadlock the manager's database pool | done | **Done 2026-11-17.** CI's `go` job hung for 15 minutes in `internal/app` (`TestBuildRunsAuthReaperAndStopsCleanly`, run 44036840359 on 8d7d4440): every background loop and `Build` itself waited in `pgxpool.Acquire`, and none held a connection that was not locked. `schedule.AdvisoryLocker` and the backup lock took their session from the shared pool and held it for the whole pass, while the locked work took further connections from the same pool. Once as many lockers held a lock as the pool had connections, each waited for a connection none would give back, and so did every request. pgx's default pool is the CPU count but at least four, there are six lockers (re-test, item expiry, retention purge, auth reaper, probe runner, backup), and the probe runner holds its lock across ticks. So a manager on a host with four CPUs or fewer could hang like this in production, not just on the four-core CI runner. With the pool capped at three connections it hung on every start. The lockers now hold their sessions on a pool of their own (`db.OpenAsRoleWithMaxConns`, `app.lockSessions` = 8, same role and application name), so a held lock never takes a connection its work needs; the backup store takes its lock session from it through `backup.NewSQLStoreWithLocks`. Tests: `TestBuildAndCloseOnASmallPool` (Build and Close on a two-connection pool, five times; it hung before the fix), `TestSQLStoreTakesTheLockSessionFromItsLockPool`, `TestOpenAsRoleWithMaxConns`. A manager now opens up to eight more sessions than before. Not this bug: run 43965412660's hang (`TestReplacingConfigsLeaksNoGoroutines` in `internal/agent/probes`, which has no database) and run 43957445728's failures; neither was analysed here |
| 3.93 | Session lock with a PIN | done | **Done 2026-11-17** (branch `feat/3.92-session-lock`). Spec: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-17-session-lock-expiry-version-design.md`. A lock icon in the top bar locks the session; the lock is enforced by the server (every route but unlock, sign-out and the session reads answers 403 `session_locked`), so a reload or a second tab stays locked, and the lock screen replaces the page instead of covering it. The PIN is 4 to 8 digits, stored as a hash, set on `/account/lock-pin` (offered once after sign-in, skippable), from the lock icon when none is set, or in the Lock PIN card on Account security (changing or removing it asks for a second factor). Five wrong PINs revoke the session and the sign-in page says so (`notice=lock_ended`). An administrator's second-factor reset also clears the account's PIN. Routes `/api/v1/auth/lock*`; refusal `lock_pin_missing` (409). The passkey step-up is now bound to the own account (`/api/v1/auth/passkeys/reauth/begin` and `finish`). Migration additive. Covered by `internal/screenlock`, the API tests and `e2e/session-lock.spec.ts` |
| 3.94 | Session expiry warning with in-place renewal | done | **Done 2026-11-17** (same branch and spec). Five minutes before the session ends a dialog shows the countdown and renews the session with the password plus a code, a recovery code or a passkey, without leaving the page (`POST /api/v1/auth/session/renew`, `/renew/passkey`). Renewal replaces the session: a new cookie, the old one is dead at once. "Later" (or Escape) collapses the dialog into a banner with the countdown and a Renew button, so the page stays usable while the person saves their work. At zero the person lands on `/login?notice=session_expired`. The remaining time is taken against the server clock learned from `/auth/me`; other tabs follow a renewal. Covered by `e2e/session-expiry.spec.ts` |
| 3.95 | New-version notice | done | **Done 2026-11-17** (same branch and spec). An open tab notices that the manager was upgraded: the public `/setup/status` is read every five minutes and when the tab returns to the foreground, and a version different from the one of the page load (the baseline is the first successful read of the load, not whatever a watcher sees first) opens "Pulsix was updated to vX" with Reload, Later and a release-notes link (`https://github.com/NoiXdev/pulsix/releases/tag/vX`, only for a version of the shape `1.2.3` or `1.2.3-rc.1`). "Later" turns it into an in-flow banner that stays until reload; failed reads change nothing. Covered by `e2e/version-notice.spec.ts` |
| 3.96 | The run's event log follows a live run | done | **Done 2026-11-17** (owner's feedback: the event log should scroll along, with a switch to turn that off). While a run is live, every new event scrolls the page's scroller (the app shell's main area) to the bottom (`web/src/features/runs/useFollowLog.ts`). A **Follow** switch sits above the log only while the run is live, on by default; the browser remembers it (`localStorage` `pulsix.runLog.follow`, read and written in try/catch). Scrolling up turns following off for that page so reading back is never interrupted, but that is not remembered; turning the switch on jumps to the newest event. A finished run never scrolls on its own, so opening one shows its result first. Docs: playbooks-and-jobs, changelog |
| 3.97 | One list component for every list | done | **Done 2026-11-17** (branch `feat/list-component`). Spec: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-17-lists-and-appearance-design.md`, plan `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-17-lists-settings-tokens.md`. Every table in the GUI is one `DataList` (`web/src/ui/data-list`) with the controller `useListView` (`web/src/features/lists`): sortable headers (a click steps ascending, descending, default order; `aria-sort`), a page footer ("1–25 of 137 hosts", page size, page buttons), a Columns menu (show, hide, reorder, restore defaults) and a row menu behind a "…" button that a right-click on the row opens too (plus "Open in new tab" and "Copy link"). Big lists page on the server (`page`, `per_page`, `sort`, `order`, answer `total`: hosts, runs, alerts, users, activity and the other cursor lists, so "Load more" is gone from every list that is not a picker); small ones sort and page in the browser. Sort, order and page live in the URL; a filter change goes back to page 1 and keeps the sort. Inline row buttons moved into the row menu (alert Acknowledge, Snooze and a timeline dialog, jump host pin and unpin of the host key, repository, template and schedule actions, alert route Enable and Disable); the hosts list gains an Active runs column ("2 running", a link to that host's live runs), a "With active runs" chip and the row entry "View active runs"; the runs status filter offers Active. Every list id and its column ids are a contract in `features/lists/registry.ts`. Migration 00109 adds the partial index `jobs_live_idx` that makes the live-run count cheap. Covered by the unit tests of each list page, `useListView`, the server paging tests and `e2e/lists.spec.ts` |
| 3.98 | Appearance settings | done | **Done 2026-11-17** (same branch and spec). Per account, in every browser: the density (comfortable, standard, compact: `<html data-density>`, the row height and type size follow; this browser keeps a hint in `localStorage` so the first paint already has it), the page size (10, 25, 50, 100) and, per list id, the chosen columns and their order. Stored in `user_preferences` (migration 00108, one row per user, no row means defaults, the list document bounded in size and shape) behind `GET` and `PUT /api/v1/auth/me/preferences` (session only). The Appearance page also lists every list with its saved column state and a Reset. Decision: a saved column list is used as it is. |
| 3.99 | Personal settings area | done | **Done 2026-11-17** (same branch). The account menu's *Settings* opens `/account/*` with a sidebar of its own (Back to the organization the person came from, then Security, Appearance, Profile, API tokens, My activity) instead of the organization shell; the organization switcher and the organization navigation are not shown there, so an instance administrator creates organizations from the organization switcher only. The Language card moved from Security to Appearance. Profile is read only. My activity lists the person's own audit events across organizations (`/account/activity`, server-paged). `/account/enrol`, `/password` and `/lock-pin` stay outside the layout. |
| 3.100 | Personal API and assistant tokens | done | **Done 2026-11-17** (same branch). Spec: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-17-personal-tokens-design.md`. A person creates their own tokens (`pxp_…`, shown once) under *Settings → API tokens*: kind API or assistant, an optional expiry, and a grant per organization with a role cap that never exceeds the person's own role (effective role is the lower of cap and current membership at every use; a removed member loses the grants). Limits: 50 tokens, 50 grants per token. Tokens are managed through a cookie session only (`/api/v1/auth/me/tokens`). Organizations: the switch *Allow personal tokens* (Settings → General, default on, `org_token_settings`), and the organization's API tokens page lists members' personal tokens with "Revoke for this organization…". An administrator's revocation is a block per person and organization: the person cannot add the organization again, their other tokens are refused there too ("Blocked (person)"), and only an administrator's Unblock lifts it. Nothing an administrator does can create or widen a grant. A missing grant answers 403 "token is scoped to another organization". Assistant tokens: `/api/mcp/o/<org>` per organization (bare `/api/mcp` only for exactly one live grant), the connect script takes `--org`. Migrations 00110 and 00111; new package `internal/personaltokens` (92 % coverage, on the 90 % gate). Covered by the service, store and API tests, `scripts/assistant-mcp-test.sh` and `e2e/personal-tokens.spec.ts` |
| 3.101 | Saved column choices hide columns added later | done | Found in the review of 3.97 (controller ruling R19). `mergeColumns` uses a saved list as it is, so a default-visible column that is added to a list later never appears for the people who saved that list (the Owner column of the organization token page is one such case for anybody who saved it before). Must be solved before a column is added to a released list, e.g. by storing the known column ids with the saved choice and showing a column that is new since the save. Nothing is released yet, so nobody has a saved list today. Done: a saved list entry now carries `known` (every column id offered at save time, at most 80, every `columns` id must be in it; no migration, the preferences are a JSON document). A choice saved without `known` is kept as it is (every column offered now counts as known), so nothing a person hid comes back. `mergeColumns` shows a default-visible column that is not in `known` at its default position and keeps a known, unchecked one hidden; an entry without `known` counts `known` as `columns` |
| 3.102 | Personal settings need an organization | done | Found in the final review of 3.97 to 3.100. A person without an organization cannot open any personal settings page: `web/src/routes/AccountLayout.tsx:53` blocks Appearance, API tokens and My activity too, though none of them needs an organization. Let the account area open without one. Done 2026-11-18: the account area opens without an organization (AppShell `org` optional; the sidebar then has no back link and no role line, the host search is hidden); the personal token dialog shows its existing "no organization" copy |
| 3.103 | Hosts filters are not in the URL | done | Found in the final review. The hosts filters (search, zone, chips, active runs) are component state while sort and page are in the URL (`HostsListPage.tsx:39–45`), so Back or a reload brings back `?page=N` of the unfiltered list. Move the filters into the URL like sort and page, or drop `page` when they are not there. Done 2026-11-18: the filters are URL search params (`features/hosts/hostsSearch.ts`: q, zone, managed, monitored, activeRuns), each change drops `page`, typing replaces the history entry |
| 3.104 | Preferences: last writer wins across tabs | done | Found in the final review. Preferences are saved as a whole document (`usePreferences.ts:32`, `staleTime: Infinity`), so a second tab or browser overwrites the first one's changes. Refetch on window focus, or save per list with a PATCH. Done: `usePreferences` refetches on window focus and on a mount once 30 s stale; saves already apply their change to the latest cached document (functional update), so a save after a refetched change carries both (test in `usePreferences.test.tsx`) |
| 3.105 | Preference saves fill "My activity" | done | Found in the final review. Every preference save is audited ("changed their appearance settings"), which buries the useful entries of My activity in noise (a column toggle is a save). Consider not auditing them, or coalescing a burst into one entry. Done: the PUT no longer carries `withAudit` (it is on the audit allowlist with the reason); nothing in preferences is security-relevant, and a test asserts a save leaves no entry in My activity |

New lock PINs are exactly four digits, typed into four boxes (`web/src/ui/pin-input.tsx`: one labelled password input lies transparent over the boxes, so paste, password managers and screen readers see one field). The lock screen posts the unlock on the fourth digit (no Unlock button); the set form has two rows, *PIN* and *Repeat the PIN*, and submits when the second matches. A PIN of unknown or older length (`lock_pin_legacy` on `/auth/me`) keeps the single field with an Unlock button and a note to set a new one under Security |
| 3.107 | One settings layout and navigation | done | **Done 2026-11-18** (same branch and spec). One sidebar (Back to the organization; My account, Organization, Alerting, Instance groups) replaces the inner navigation of the organization settings; every settings card page is one column of cards (`section[data-card]` with a title); forms save through a footer bar (Save disabled until something changed, *Unsaved changes* and Discard while dirty) and standalone switches apply at once. Password change, authenticator setup, recovery codes, adding a passkey and set/change PIN open dialogs; the recovery codes dialog can only be closed with *I have saved these*. Member and account pages have Account, Role, Sign-in, Actions and Danger zone cards |

| 3.111 | A backup save kept the typed secret key for seconds | done | Found by the owner 2026-11-18 ("secret key wird immer beim Speichern neu geschrieben"). The save waited for the backup list to reload, which lists the remote storage and can take seconds; until then the destination card still held the typed secret and counted as unsaved, so a second Save sent the key again. The save now waits only for the settings (the list reloads in the background); measured in the browser: clean after ~60 ms instead of ~3 s. The S3 secret and SMTP password fields also tell password managers not to fill or save them (a filled-in login password would otherwise be sent as the secret). Test connection already probes the destination as entered, before saving, and the card says so |
| 3.112 | Host page with header and tabs, SSH access dialog | done | **Done 2026-11-18** (branch `feat/host-view-tabs`). Spec: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-18-host-view-tabs-design.md`. The host page has a header (name, state pill, actions) and tabs (Overview, Checks, Alerts, Runs, Inventory & facts, Connection) kept in the URL as `?tab=`; the breadcrumb ends with the host's name. The Checks tab is a compact table (source, interval, worst verdict, key value; failing rows first, crit before warn, the first failing row open) whose rows open to the readings; the Overview and the table share one verdict rule (a disabled check or a stale verdict reads unknown) and mute it for an offline client (3.57). SSH access is a dialog over any tab: address, port, user, credential, jump host with the route preview, key snippets and "Save and test". The connection test cannot test unsaved values (the worker resolves the route from the saved settings), so the dialog saves first and then tests, and follows the run in place. Agent list links open the Connection tab (`?tab=connection`). |
| 3.113 | Optional second factor: superseded sessions in the admin session count; no sign-in stamp after re-verifying | done | Done 2026-11-24 on `fix/small-items-3.65-3.113-3.121-3.122` (`4b215186`): a re-verify flips the same session row, so the admin count and the sign-in stamp go by the row's `password_only` and whether the account has a factor: a password-only session superseded by a factor no longer counts, and signing in with a factor (TOTP verify, passkey assertion) stamps `users.last_sign_in_at`; enrolling a first factor from a password-only session completes it without stamping (`a6aafb48`). Real-DB tests with the mode on and off, shown failing on the old queries |
| 3.114 | Optional second factor: three small follow-ups from the last security review | done | Found 2026-11-18 in the final re-review of the optional second factor (verdict: safe to merge). (1) The password-only sign-in marker can stay set on an account that got a factor between login's factor check and the session insert; a later first factor (after an admin reset) then revokes the owner's newer tokens too — errs on the safe side. (2) The org-token insert runs in an instance transaction through the org_isolation_bypass policy (`internal/api/token_handlers.go:160-171`), so RLS no longer checks it; run the lock and re-check inside `WithWriteTx(orgID)` instead. (3) A pending (not password-only) session's first enrolment can still finish after another session added a factor (main has the same race); now that first enrolments serialise, refuse when the account is enrolled and the session is not complete. Done 2026-11-18: (1) `CreateSession` marks the account only when it has no factor in the insert's snapshot, and the second-factor reset (`DeleteSecondFactors`) drops a mark it finds beside a factor, which closes the window of a factor still in flight; (2) `Enrolment.WithCompleteSession` takes the organization and runs in `WithWriteTx(orgID)`, so RLS refuses a token of another organization; (3) finishing an enrolment from a pending session of an account that has a factor is `ErrSessionNotComplete` (403), so of two pending sessions enrolling at once only the first completes |
| 3.115 | Group page with header, tabs, manage-fields dialog, inheritance view and JSON viewer | done | **Done 2026-11-18** (branch `feat/groups-and-list-filters`). Header instead of the sidebar; tabs Variables · Members · Subgroups · Inheritance · Inventory in `?tab=` (old tabs and anchors redirect; unsaved values survive tab switches); Variables shows where each effective value comes from and saves through the card save bar; Manage fields is a dialog (readable rows with type/scope/required badges, a field panel for all nine schema types, More settings, Assign preview); the Inheritance table follows how the inventory is rendered (a group's schema default counts at its own level; no vars on `all`; same-depth groups by name) and is cross-checked against a golden inventory; the inventory preview uses the new `JsonViewer` (`web/src/ui/json-viewer.tsx`). |

| 3.117 | Forgetting an item or a metric leaves its per-vantage probe rows | done | Done 2026-11-24 on `fix/small-items-3.73-3.117-3.118` (`844347c5`): the item forget and the metric forget delete the matching `probe_current` rows in the same transaction; a real-DB test shows `VantageCurrent` no longer lists the forgotten metric while other metrics' rows survive |
| 3.118 | Metrics momentarily vanished during a partial replay after a long gap | done | Done 2026-11-24 on `fix/small-items-3.73-3.117-3.118` (`a19a823a`, `b3d8c053`), no protocol or migration change: a host counts as replaying while none of its agent's checks has a reading at or after `host_agents.online_since` (reset after any gap over 90 s, before the replay starts) and less than 15 minutes (the agent's disconnect buffer window) have passed; meanwhile its would-be-vanished tuples are held out of the evaluation, so no alert closes as `stale` and reopens. The first new-period reading on any item ends the hold for all of them, so an item that never reports again still vanishes. Manager probe readings (`CheckContext.Synthetic`) neither end nor are held by a replay. Left: the host page shows such a row as vanished for the seconds a replay lasts; a gap under 90 s with one collection over 200 results split across chunks is not covered; a reconnect whose stream-open touch fails does not reset `online_since` and gets no hold |
| 3.119 | Manage a host's inventory groups from the host page | done | **Done 2026-11-19.** The host page's Inventory tab (`HostInventoryCard`) now offers, for roles with `inventory.write` (the group page's rule), **Add to group …** (the shared `AssignHostDialog` with the host fixed and a new `GroupPicker` instead of the host picker: groups the host is already in are not offered, `all` is not a listed group; the form follows the chosen group), **Edit values …** per direct row (same dialog in member mode, reading the group for its schema) and **Remove from group …** (confirm naming host and group, `useUnassignHost`). The mutations already invalidate the whole `['inventory', org]` tree, so the host's groups and the group's members refresh. Membership problems show on the row (`N to fill in`). Inherited groups are shown: ancestors of the direct groups, taken from the groups list's `parent_id`, muted with "via <subgroup>", no actions. Readers see the list as before; unmanaged hosts keep the management-off hint. No e2e selectors touched. |
| 3.120 | A real code editor for playbooks and other code fields | done | **Done 2026-11-19.** New presentational `web/src/ui/code-editor.tsx` (`CodeEditor`: value/onChange, language `yaml`/`json`/`text`, label or labelledBy, readOnly, invalid, placeholder, maxLength, height/minHeight/maxHeight, diagnostics with an optional 1-based line). CodeMirror 6 (`@codemirror/state` 6.7.6, `view` 6.43.15, `commands` 6.11.1, `language` 6.13.1, `lang-yaml` 6.1.3, `lang-json` 6.0.2, `search` 6.7.2, `lint` 6.9.7, `@lezer/highlight` 1.2.5, exact pins; `style-mod` 4.1.4 as a direct dependency plus the override `$style-mod`, because 4.1.5 was two days old and the override alone nested two copies; see `docs/dependency-exceptions.md`, liftable from 2026-12-01), loaded lazily as its own chunk (`ui/codemirror/CodeMirrorEditor.tsx`, about 395 kB / 128.8 kB gzip) so the entry chunk only grows by the wrapper; the Suspense fallback and the error-boundary fallback (a failed chunk after a deploy) are the plain monospace textarea with the same name. Line numbers, active line, bracket matching, folding, search (Mod-F), undo/redo, Tab/Shift-Tab indent by two spaces (no tab characters), auto-indent on Enter, no soft wrap, JetBrains Mono, colours only from CSS variables (two new tokens `--px-syntax-string`/`--px-syntax-number`, which the JSON viewer now uses too). Escape then Tab leaves the editor (CodeMirror's tab-focus mode), said in a hint under every editable editor; the first Escape in the text is taken so a dialog does not close, a second one closes it (read-only editors never take Escape; a second Escape must come within two seconds). The playbook dialog opens with the focus in the YAML (`autoFocus`), Escape leaves full screen first and then asks about unsaved changes on the keydown (so no close request is spent), `onCancel` stays as the fallback for focus on the dialog chrome; the hint shows only once CodeMirror is mounted, not under the textarea stand-in. Fields now on the editor: the playbook editor (YAML, 60 % of the viewport, full-screen toggle, Ctrl/Cmd-S saves when the preview passes, unsaved changes ask before Cancel/Escape close the dialog, the tab-indentation error marked on its line via the new `validateYAMLProblem`; other validator messages carry no line), the ad-hoc command (text, keeps the 8 KiB cap) and a schedule's extra vars (JSON, read-only for readers). Stayed textareas: check list params and assertions (`checks/ParamFields.tsx`, `AssertionEditor`: one entry per line, not JSON), rule expressions and labels, webhook body template (JSON with `{{ }}` placeholders), email recipients, enum options in the variables builder (`variables/inputs.tsx` has no JSON input), private keys and credentials, and plain-text notes (acknowledge, maintenance, item decisions). e2e: the playbook fills in `ansible`, `chain` and `variables` specs go through the new `e2e/codeEditor.ts` helper (`fill` on `.cm-content`). jsdom got `Range.getClientRects`/`getBoundingClientRect` stubs in `src/test/setup.ts`. |
| 3.121 | The field-manage dialog closes on a third Escape without asking | done | Done 2026-11-24 on `fix/small-items-3.65-3.113-3.121-3.122` (`fd24c24c`): the fields dialog and the host SSH dialog ask on Escape through one shared hook (`features/shared/useAskOnEscape.ts`, `b970d414`) that listens on `document`, so the question comes even when a click on neutral dialog content left the focus on `<body>` (the final review found the wrapper `onKeyDown` missed that); `onCancel` stays the fields dialog's fallback (the SSH dialog has none, as before); repeated Escapes fire no unrefusable `cancel` |
| 3.122 | Code editor: three deferred minors | done | Done 2026-11-24 on `fix/small-items-3.65-3.113-3.121-3.122` (`fd24c24c`). (1) superseded by 3.123. (2) `CodeEditor`'s `maxLength` cuts an oversize paste or insert to the room left, like a textarea. (3) clicking the label of the ad-hoc command or the schedule's extra vars focuses the editor (`CodeEditor` exposes `focus()`) |
| 3.123 | Playbooks on pages of their own | done | **Done 2026-11-19** (branch `feat/playbook-pages`, spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-19-playbook-pages-design.md` §1). The playbook dialog (`PlaybookEditor`) is gone; the list's rows link to `/o/$org/playbooks/$id` and **New playbook** opens `/o/$org/playbooks/new`. The playbook page: a header with the name (mono), the pills *Valid* or the preview's problem, *Unsaved* and the source (*Editor* or `<repo> @ <branch>`, the list's marker), `<summary> · updated <when>`, **Run…** (always a dialog to pick the template, the only one preselected, then the templates list's flow, now `templates/useRunTemplate.tsx`; with a dirty draft it says the saved version runs; none: a link to creating a template with this playbook), **Save** (disabled until the draft is dirty and passes the preview; Ctrl/Cmd-S anywhere on the Editor tab) and **⋯ → Delete…** (disabled while templates use it or are still being read, the reason in the item; the server's 409 still toasts; the deleted playbook is not read again on the way out). Tabs in `?tab=` (`editor` default, `runs`, `used`); the draft lives above them (while clean it follows the stored text, so someone else's save is not reverted) and the Editor panel stays mounted, so a tab switch keeps it, and leaving with it asks (`useUnsavedGuard` with `samePath`). Editor tab: the shared `CodeEditor` at page height and a side column with **Check** (the preview's message and line, the server's refusal of the last save), **Variables** (a hint: the top-level names of the YAML's `{{ }}` expressions, each *set in vars* by the play using it, *set by a task* via `register`/`set_fact`, or *needs a value from the template or inventory*; Ansible's own names and loop variables left out; `playbooks/variables.ts`, line-based, table-tested) and **Latest runs** (3, a link to the Runs tab). Runs tab: the runs list with the server's new `playbook_id` filter, status/trigger/template filters and server paging (list `playbook-runs`, state in the component). Used by tab: the templates using it (client-side filter), their limit, schedules and webhook triggers (one request each per listed template), the templates list's row menu and edit dialog (a reader: the template's variables page); **Create template…** opens the new template dialog with the playbook chosen (`TemplateDialog`'s `initialPlaybookId`, reached through the templates page's `?create=<playbook>`). A repository playbook has no text in Pulsix: a note names the path, repository and branch and says changes come through Git, no Save. A reader sees the YAML read-only. The new page: *Write in the editor* (named by its first play's `name:`, four starters: empty play, install packages, file from a template, restart a service; replacing edited text asks; leaving with edited text asks, whichever source is shown) or *Choose from a repository* (repository, then a discovered path; registers it or opens the one already registered), then the playbook's page; without `playbooks.write` the new page is the no-access notice. Breadcrumbs name the playbook and the new page. e2e (`ansible`, `chain`, `variables`) create playbooks through `createEditorPlaybook` in `e2e/codeEditor.ts`. Item (1) of 3.122 went with the dialog. |

Rows with a page already had the row link. Converted to dialog activation: checks, item decisions, monitoring groups, rules, maintenance windows, alert routes, channels, rotations, repositories, templates, secrets, jump hosts and organization tokens (edit dialog, writers only; an expired token not), personal tokens (not expired), credentials (details dialog, everyone; replaces the name cell's own button) and alerts (timeline, everyone).
