The agent's disk plugin leaves out every mount point it cannot name inside the manager's contract (`reportableItem`: at most `maxItemLen` = 512 bytes, no control character, U+2028 or U+2029). A discovered mount is skipped silently. An explicitly configured one is skipped and named in the plugin's error. One odd mount therefore no longer gets the host's whole batch refused or its stream cut. The manager's all-or-nothing refusal is unchanged: a violating item still means a broken or lying agent. Tests: TestDiskSkipsMountsTheManagerWouldRefuse (a 517-byte, a TAB and a U+2028 mount beside /; only / is reported, no error) and TestReportableItemMatchesTheManagersContract (pins maxItemLen to internal/checks.MaxItemLen). Finding: Lead, no severity. The check-results item contract (at most 512 bytes, no control or line-separator characters) is enforced only at manager ingest. It is an all-or-nothing batch refusal that also ends the agent stream (samples.go:283-286, stream.go:266-275 and 106-111). The design comment (samples.go:102-125) knows that items are mount points, but it treats any violation as 'the agent is broken or lying'. It does not consider that a … Blocker: Can an unprivileged local user on a supported Linux distribution create a mount in init's mount namespace whose path is longer than 512 bytes or contains a TAB or newline, for example sshfs/FUSE through setuid fusermount3 in a directory they own, or udisks … Fingerprint `internal/checks/samples.go:withinContract:single-disk-mount-item-refuses-whole-agent-batch` |
| 7.41 | Host-tag selectors have no length or condition limit and become one huge SQL statement on the shared pool | done | **Hardened 2026-11-02 without measuring Postgres.** `selector.Parse` is the one entry every selector passes, whether a tag selector, a monitoring group, a preview or a stored one re-read on resolve. It now refuses a selector longer than `MaxLen` (4096 bytes) before lexing and one with more than `MaxConditions` (32) conditions, so the statement Selector.SQL compiles carries at most 32 sub-queries. The write paths already map a parse error to a 4xx. Upgrade note: a stored selector over these bounds, which the UI never produces, would stop resolving and fail its organization's resolve like any unparsable stored selector. Test: TestParseBoundsTheSelector (32 accepted, 33 refused, an over-long in-list refused for its length). Finding: Lead, no severity. Neither the selector grammar (parser.go:56-72), the request DTOs (tag_handlers.go:90 has only minLength:1; monitoring_group_handlers.go:37 and :67 have no constraint), checks.normalize (groups.go:240-243) nor checks.Preview (groups.go:337) bound selector length or condition count before Selector.SQL (selector.go:184-219) compiles it into one EXISTS sub-query per condition that hosts.SQLStore.QueryHosts executes as a … Blocker: The decisive cost, PostgreSQL parse/plan/execute time and memory for one statement with 1000 to 65000 correlated EXISTS sub-queries (pulled up into nested semi-joins) over a materialized CTE, cannot be measured: DB-backed execution is forbidden in this … Fingerprint `internal/hosts/selector/parser.go:Parse:unbounded-selector-conditions-to-sql` |
| 7.42 | Host agent can still inject chat mentions and masked phishing links into alert notifications via check-result item names | done | **Hardened 2026-11-02 without settling the provider facts.** Whether a chat service renders the markup no longer decides it. The text form of alerts.items (used by `{{ alerts.items }}` in webhook bodies and by the e-mail body) now passes an alert's item, the one field the monitored host's agent chooses, through `agentText`: '@', '<', '>', '[' and ']' become look-alikes no chat service reads as markup (U+FF20, U+2039, U+203A, U+FF3B, U+FF3D), and Unicode format characters (category Cf: bidi overrides, zero-width marks) become U+FFFD, like control characters already did. An ordinary item is unchanged. The structured `| json` form keeps the raw value, since a template author who picks it handles the data themselves. Test: TestAlertItemsTextDefusesAnAgentsItemName (mention, Slack special, Slack link, Markdown link, RLO, zero-width; failed before). Finding: Lead, no severity. Agent-chosen check_key/item/metric are bounded only by length and by breaksLine/oneLine (control characters, U+2028/U+2029). Neither ingest (checks.withinContract) nor render (notify.alertItemsText/oneLine/escapedText) neutralises chat-markup metacharacters ('@' mentions, '<!...>' and '<url/text>', '[text](url)') or Unicode format/bidi characters before the value is placed into the alerts.items text that … Blocker: Whether Discord/Slack/Teams (or the operator's chosen chat endpoint) render '@everyone', '<!channel>', '[text](url)' or '<url/text>' from a webhook body built with {{ alerts.items }} as a live mention or masked link is a third-party provider fact not … Fingerprint `internal/notify/render.go:alertItemsText:agent-item-chat-markup` |
| 7.43 | Outbound webhook policy blocks only AWS's IPv6 metadata address; other providers' IPv6 metadata endpoints in fc00::/7 are dialled | done | **Hardened 2026-11-02 without settling the provider facts.** Whether a provider's IPv6 endpoint answers a webhook's method no longer decides it. `blockedRanges` refuses each provider's whole IPv6 instance-service /64 as cloud metadata: `fd00:ec2::/64` (AWS; it replaced the single IMDS address and also covers the DNS resolver and time service beside it), `fd20:ce::/64` (Google Cloud metadata fd20:ce::254) and `fd00:c1::/64` (Oracle Cloud metadata fd00:c1::a9fe:a9fe). The rest of fc00::/7 stays allowed as self-hosted private space, and `PULSIX_NOTIFY_ALLOW_CIDRS` can still re-admit a range. Spec §14 lists the ranges. Test: TestPolicyRefusesIPv6CloudInstanceServices (the five addresses refused, neighbouring unique-local space allowed; four of the five passed before). Finding: Lead, no severity. blockedRanges() (internal/outbound/policy.go:31-55) implements metadata protection as a short list of literal addresses whose only IPv6 entry is AWS's fd00:ec2::254/128. That list mirrors the architecture spec's enumeration (2026-09-10-pulsix-architecture-design.md:727-728). fc00::/7 is otherwise allowed wholesale as self-hosted private space, and the embedded-IPv4 extractors (policy.go:73-138, nat64.go) do not … Blocker: The IPv6 metadata endpoint addresses (GCE fd20:ce::254, OCI fd00:c1::a9fe:a9fe, AWS fd00:ec2::23) and the methods and paths they accept are provider facts, not repository facts. Examples are whether GCE answers POST at all and whether a guest-attributes PUT … Fingerprint `internal/outbound/policy.go:blockedRanges:ipv6-cloud-metadata-endpoints-unlisted` |
| 7.44 | Security audit run 3: cover what run 2 deferred | done | **Ran 2026-11-03** (scoped to run 2's gaps and every change since 4f9c7103, 48 agent invocations): `docs/security/2026-11-03-audit-run-3/`, follow-ups 7.49–7.62. Run 2 deferred 4 units for budget (critics c02/c03): agent-reported fact and check-item cardinality in persisted org state; the zone worker's certificate renewal and reconnect client; the shared rule-evaluation ticker under one organization's rules and selectors; the cross-organization `/me/activity` feed after a membership ends (no run reviewed `internal/audit/store_sql.go`). Five run-1 covered units with unchanged source were not re-passed (agent installer, notification delivery policy, repository definitions, unscoped tickers, worker enrollment). Start from run 2's ledger (`~/security-audit-skill/pulsix/run-2`) |
| 7.45 | A key rotation does not remove the old key from the credential's own account when runs log in as a different one | done | **Fixed 2026-11-02.** The revocation names the bootstrap account as well: `revokeKindPayload` adds `also_users` = [the credential's username, or `credentials.DefaultLoginUser` when it has none; `bootstrapAccount`, which the bootstrap payload now uses too] whenever that differs from the account runs log in as. The worker passes it on as `pulsix_also_users` (a JSON list, empty rather than missing). `pulsix.revoke_key.yml` looks every named account up with getent, requires the run account to exist, and removes the previous key from each account that exists, matching any authorized_keys line that carries the key's base64 blob as a word, whatever options or comment surround it. A missing extra account is no failure. Tests: TestRevocationAlsoNamesTheCredentialsOwnAccount (jobs), TestRevokeExecutorPassesTheOtherAccounts (worker), TestRevokePlaybookClearsTheOtherAccountsToo (the embedded playbook as root on the worker image's ansible-core: both accounts lose the old key, keep the other one, and a missing account passes). Writing that test found 7.46. Finding: Follow-up of 7.33 (2026-11-02). Since 7.33 a rotation installs, verifies and revokes on the account runs log in as. Bootstrap, however, installs the credential's key for the credential's own username (\`bootstrapKindPayload\`'s \`pulsix_user\`). So on a host whose own SSH username differs from the credential's, the bootstrapped key can also sit in the credential's account, and a rotation leaves it there. The fix direction from audit run 2: the revocation also removes the old key from the credential's own account where that account exists. That needs \`pulsix.revoke_key.yml\` to skip an account that does not exist (an \`ansible.builtin.getent\` lookup before \`authorized_key\`), a second account in the revoke payload, and a real-ansible test, since the fake runner cannot show the module's behaviour on a missing user. Low: it needs the operator to have run bootstrap for one account and then pointed the host at another. |
| 7.46 | The system playbooks (bootstrap, rotate_key, revoke_key) called a module ansible-core does not ship, so none of them could run in the worker image | done | **Found and fixed 2026-11-02, while writing 7.45's real-ansible test.** All three embedded playbooks (\`internal/worker/playbooks\`) used \`ansible.builtin.authorized_key\`. That module lives in the ansible.posix collection, and the worker image installs ansible-core 2.21.4 without collections. ansible-playbook therefore refused each of them at load ("couldn't resolve module/action 'ansible.builtin.authorized_key'"), before any task ran: one-time bootstrap and SSH key rotation could not succeed on the shipped worker. The suite never saw it, because the fake runner does not run playbooks and the real-ansible e2e mode covers no system job. The playbooks now use only ansible-core's own modules: \`getent\` for the account's home, \`file\` for an owner-only .ssh (0700), \`lineinfile\` to add the key (authorized_keys 0600) or to remove every line carrying the old key's blob. Bootstrap still creates the account and the sudo rule. Installing the ansible.posix collection into the image was rejected, to keep the image free of an extra download and dependency. Tests run each embedded playbook as root on the worker image's own ansible-core (\`internal/e2e/revoke_playbook_test.go\`: TestBootstrapPlaybookCreatesTheAccountWithTheKey, TestRotatePlaybookInstallsTheNewKey, TestRevokePlaybookClearsTheOtherAccountsToo). All three failed with the load error before the change. |

## v1.0 · security audit run 3 follow-ups

From `docs/security/2026-11-03-audit-run-3/` (2026-11-03, `main` at 7ae1808, scoped to run 2's gaps and the changes since 4f9c7103). 7.49–7.53 are confirmed (reproduced locally and verified twice independently), all low; 7.54–7.60 are leads that need a database, a deployment fact or an owner decision, without severity; 7.61–7.62 are units the run deferred for budget. 7.37 (release secrets) is still open and unchanged. Decisions taken with the owner on 2026-11-03 are in each row.

| # | Item | Status | Notes |
|---|------|--------|-------|
| 7.49 | An API token that may not mint tokens can still mint worker and agent enrollment tokens whose identities survive its revocation | done | **Fixed 2026-11-03.** Both creates call `requireInteractiveSession` before `requireOrg`; listing and withdrawing stay open to tokens. Confirmed, low. `POST /orgs/{org}/worker-enrollments` (admin) and `POST /orgs/{org}/agents/tokens` (operator) sit on the bearer-enabled org group and call `requireOrg` only, so a `pxt_` token mints a worker or agent identity that keeps working after the token is revoked: the variant 7.31 left open. **Decision:** both creates require an interactive session, like token and membership minting; listing and withdrawing stay open to tokens. Automated agent roll-out by API token is given up on purpose |
| 7.50 | Forged plaintext backup headers make scheduled retention delete every authentic backup, including the one just written | done | **Fixed 2026-11-03.** The header carries an HMAC-SHA256 keyed by `HeaderKey` (HKDF label `pulsix backup header v1`), the manifest repeats it and `Verify` compares it; `ParseObjectName` accepts only canonical names (fractional-second aliases were a bypass found in review); `Prune` counts only verified, past-dated scheduled objects and never the run's own backup. Backups made before this fix list as unverified, still download and restore, and are never pruned — the manager logs how many it kept on each prune run. Confirmed, low. `Prune` decides from the unauthenticated plaintext header (`CreatedAt`, `Kind`), so anyone who can only add objects to the backup folder or bucket makes the manager delete every real scheduled backup on each run. **Fix:** prune always keeps the backup the run just wrote; the header carries a MAC keyed by an HKDF of the master key (own label); an object whose MAC is missing or wrong counts as unverified, is never pruned and never fills a tier; the name's stamp and kind must match the header; objects claiming a creation time after the run are ignored |
| 7.51 | `restore --list` writes terminal control sequences from unauthenticated backup headers and object names | done | **Fixed 2026-11-03.** `restore --list` prints names and header strings through `printable`; `ReadHeader` refuses unknown kinds and unprintable versions. Confirmed, low. Escape non-printable runes in every name and header string `restore --list` prints; `ReadHeader` refuses a kind other than scheduled/manual and a non-printable `pulsix_version` (such objects list as damaged) |
| 7.52 | A mount point with a quote and a non-UTF-8 byte still poisons the agent's stream after 7.40 | done | **Fixed 2026-11-03.** `reportableItem` and `withinContract` require valid UTF-8; the agent session drops a chunk that fails client-side marshalling instead of re-buffering it. Confirmed, low. gopsutil keeps the raw bytes when unquoting fails, proto3 cannot encode the item, and the agent re-buffers and replays the same message on every reconnect, so the host goes silent while it still looks seen. **Fix:** `reportableItem` and the manager's `withinContract` require valid UTF-8; the agent session drops a chunk it cannot encode instead of re-buffering it |
| 7.53 | Run-output redaction misses a secret containing both quote kinds when a play prints it inside a container interpolated into a string | done | **Fixed 2026-11-03.** `newRedactor` adds the CPython repr spellings (the secret's own quote choice and the always-single-quote form) and their JSON forms. Confirmed, low (reproduced with ansible-core 2.21.4 in the worker image). Python repr escapes `'` as `\'` when both quote kinds occur; `newRedactor` gains repr spellings and their JSON-escaped forms |
| 7.54 | A restore reactivates sessions, API tokens, certificates and memberships revoked after the backup | done | **Fixed 2026-11-03 as decided.** The load deletes every session; the restore output, `deploy/compose/README.md` and the end-user docs name what stays at the backup's state (API tokens, certificates, memberships, disabled users, passwords, passkeys, second factors and recovery codes, open invitations). Agent and worker enrollment tokens also come back (a used or withdrawn token is redeemable until it expires): the end-user docs say so, the restore output does not yet. Lead (owner decision). **Decision:** a restore ends every browser session (everyone signs in again); API tokens, worker/agent certificates, memberships and disabled users stay at the backup's state, and the restore output, `deploy/compose/README.md` and the end-user docs say so and what to check afterwards. The README's "a revoked worker's certificate stops working at once" gets the restore caveat |
| 7.55 | Restore checks for a running manager only once, before the confirmation and verification | done | **Fixed 2026-11-03.** `RestoreOptions.BeforeWipe` rechecks for managers right before the wipe; `load` share-locks and refuses non-empty `users`/`organizations`/`org_memberships` with `ErrWrittenDuringRestore`, which the CLI explains (stop every manager, rerun with --replace). Lead (needs Postgres to time). **Decision:** harden without measuring: check for managers again right before the wipe, and make the load refuse when `users`, `organizations` or `org_memberships` already hold rows |
| 7.56 | One host agent can grow `hosts.facts` without bound | done | **Fixed 2026-11-03.** `RecordFacts` enforces the agent's key contract; `UpsertHostFacts` replaces instead of merging when the merged set would exceed `MaxStoredFacts` (128). Lead (needs Postgres to measure). **Decision:** bound it: the manager enforces the agent's own fact-key contract (`[a-z0-9_]`, 1–32 characters) and a host's stored facts are capped at 128 keys (a push that would exceed it replaces the stored set instead of merging) |
| 7.57 | One host agent can persist unbounded check tuples that every evaluation of that host re-reads | done | **Fixed 2026-11-03.** Update-first ingest with a per-batch count; new tuples past `MaxTuplesPerHost` (4096) are dropped and counted in `Ingest.OverCap`. Two concurrent batches can overshoot by one batch. Lead (needs Postgres to measure). **Decision:** cap distinct (check_key, item, metric) tuples per host at 4096; observations for new tuples past the cap are dropped and counted, existing tuples keep updating |
| 7.58 | One tenant's window-heavy rule can starve the instance-wide rule evaluation ticker | done | **Fixed 2026-11-03.** `MaxWindowsPerRule` (16) at save; `SET LOCAL statement_timeout = '10s'` per host; a failed host gets `check_eval_cursor.retry_after = now()+60s` (migration 00076) and both claim queries skip it until then — a `next_due_at` postpone, as first planned, still let a dirty failed host sort ahead of healthy ones. Lead (needs TimescaleDB to time). **Decision:** at most 16 distinct window aggregates per rule expression (refused at save); each host's evaluation runs with a 10 s statement timeout; a host whose evaluation fails is postponed 60 s instead of heading the next batch again |
| 7.59 | The compose worker snippet writes the live enrollment token into the compose file; the README still shows the token as a `docker run` argument | done | **Fixed 2026-11-03.** The compose YAML reads `${PULSIX_ENROLL_TOKEN:-}` (an empty default, so later `ps`/`down`/upgrades still work) and the create response carries `compose_up_command`; the README example uses the environment form with `--init` and the play uid. Lead (deployment fact). **Decision:** the compose YAML no longer contains the token (it reads `${PULSIX_ENROLL_TOKEN}` from the shell) and the dialog shows the start command that supplies it; `deploy/compose/README.md` uses the same environment form as the docker snippet |
| 7.60 | Webhook policy still dials the IPv6 metadata addresses of Akamai/Linode and Scaleway | done | **Fixed 2026-11-03** as decided. Lead (provider facts). **Decision:** add `fd00:a9fe:a9fe::/64` (Akamai/Linode, `fd00:a9fe:a9fe::1`) and `fd00:42::/64` (Scaleway, `fd00:42::42`) to the blocked ranges; the rest of fc00::/7 stays allowed |
| 7.61 | One unreadable or special entry in the backup folder or bucket fails or blocks every backup listing, and prune runs that listing under the instance-wide backup lock | done | **Fixed 2026-11-03.** The folder listing skips non-regular entries and opens with `O_NOFOLLOW|O_NONBLOCK`; a per-entry error becomes that object's `HeaderErr` in both backends; `Open` also refuses non-regular files (a symlinked backup can no longer be downloaded or restored). Deferred by the run (final-clean critic). **Decision:** harden: the folder listing skips non-regular files (no open on a FIFO or symlink), and an entry that cannot be read or described is listed as damaged instead of failing the whole listing, in both backends |
| 7.62 | An agent's oversized facts could make a job's claim response exceed the 64 MiB worker limit | done | **Fixed 2026-11-03.** Today's behaviour was wrong: gRPC refused the oversize response after the claim had committed and the job looped through the reaper. `ClaimJob` now checks the size and fails the job at once with an error naming size and limit. Deferred by the run (post-wave critic), never reviewed. **Decision:** check what an oversize claim response does to the committed claim and the org's queue; if it loops or blocks, fail that job with a clear error instead. 7.56's cap is the main mitigation |
| 7.63 | Leftovers from the 7.49–7.62 reviews | done | **Done 2026-11-03.** Restore output names enrollment tokens and every re-enroll case; `runHost` logs say postponing only with a live context; the agent drops results with invalid UTF-8 in check_key, item, metric or label and sanitises error text; test hygiene. (1) The restore output does not yet name agent and worker enrollment tokens, which a restore makes redeemable again until they expire (the end-user docs do). (2) `internal/rules/due_sql.go` `runHost` logs "postponing" on a commit failure or panic even when the batch was cut short and nothing is postponed. (3) The agent could check `utf8.ValidString` on every result before sending, so the session's drop of an unencodable chunk stays a backstop; a drop can lose chunks already queued on that stream. (4) Minor test and comment hygiene listed in the branch's reviews (e.g. the displaced `panicStore` doc comment in `internal/rules/ticker_test.go`, the vacuous size regex in the oversize-claim test) |
| 7.64 | Leftovers from the 7.3/7.47/7.48 reviews | done | Package `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-05-7x-leftovers.md`, branch `fix/7.x-leftovers` (2026-11-05). Items 1, 2, 4 and 5 were already done on 2026-11-03 (`0f3b667`, `ec1f2a5`, `3b3f249`, `c30bd1f`) and verified again. Item 3: `go test -race -count=20 ./internal/backup/` ran clean (0 data races, 1 790 s; a single run takes about 90 s on a loaded machine, so the 900 s timeout of the brief is too short for 20 runs) — not reproduced, closed without a change |

## v1.0 · security audit run 4 follow-ups

From `docs/security/2026-11-09-audit-run-4/` (2026-11-09, `main` at 52f27625, scoped to run 3's deferred units, the revalidation of run 3's records and every change since 7ae1808). Every run-3 fix (7.49–7.62) holds. 7.101–7.106 are confirmed (reproduced locally and verified twice independently): one medium, five low; 7.107–7.114 are leads that need a database, a deployment fact or an owner decision, without severity. The owner asked on 2026-11-10 for all of them to be worked on; the fixes are planned in `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-10-audit-run-4-fixes.md`.

| # | Item | Status | Notes |
|---|------|--------|-------|
| 7.101 | A worker that already holds a clone keeps fetching from the old origin after the repository URL changes | done | **Fixed 2026-11-10.** Confirmed, medium. `internal/worker/runner/gitrepo.go` `fetchGit` runs `git fetch` against the clone's stored `origin` on the update path, so a changed URL never takes effect on a worker that cloned before: the old source keeps supplying the playbooks, and the current git token or ssh key is offered to it (the ssh host-key pin limits the ssh case). **Fix:** the update path fetches from the URL the claim carries (`git fetch -- <spec.URL> <branch>`) or re-clones on a mismatch; regression test with a second fetch under a different URL. **Done:** `cbf4b7ab`. The update path now fetches from the URL the claim carries and `remote.origin.url` follows it; no re-clone, as proposed. A clone whose stored origin already equals the claim URL stays incremental. Fingerprint `internal/worker/runner/gitrepo.go:fetchGit:update-fetches-stale-origin` |
| 7.102 | Forgot-password hands out a reset link right after an admin's second-factor reset | done | **Fixed 2026-11-10.** Confirmed, low. After `ResetSecondFactor` without a temporary password the account has a password and no factor; `RequestReset` then mails a link, so whoever holds the mailbox owns the account before its owner re-enrols. **Fix:** self-service forgot issues no link while the account has no confirmed second factor (read under `LockAccount`'s row lock with the `has_second_factor` expression `ListAccounts` already uses; map it in `SQLStore.LockAccount`, `internal/accounts/store_sql.go:140`, or forgot stops working for everyone); the admin's reset link and temporary password stay. **Done:** `34ba0ddf`. As proposed: `LockAccount` reports `HasSecondFactor` (a confirmed TOTP secret or a passkey) and `requestReset` issues no link and sends no mail while it is false, answering 202 `{}` exactly as for an unknown address. Accounts that never enrolled a factor are included on purpose; their recovery is the administrator's reset link or temporary password, which `SendResetLink` still issues unchanged. Fingerprint `internal/accounts/reset.go:requestReset:forgot-link-after-admin-second-factor-reset` |
| 7.103 | Repository URLs may embed credentials, which are stored in plain text and shown to every viewer and every organization a shared definition reaches | done | **Fixed 2026-11-10.** Confirmed, low. `internal/repos/service.go` `validateURL` and the shared-definition create/update accept `https://user:token@host/…`. **Fix:** refuse userinfo with a password or a bare token at save (organization repositories and shared definitions, one exported validator; a bare user stays allowed for ssh and scp-style URLs) and point at a git credential; redact userinfo in the repository DTOs for rows stored earlier; no row rewrite (migrations stay additive), an upgrade note tells owners to rotate such tokens. **Done:** `8402ce8c`, `b16de66a`. As proposed (`repos.ValidateURL` / `URLProblem` is the one validator, `RedactURL` hides stored userinfo in the repository and definition DTOs, no row rewrite), with three additions: a bare user on https is refused too (it is a token in the user slot; a bare user stays on ssh and scp-style URLs); the shared-definition service also gained the scheme, leading-dash, white-space and length rules it lacked; and `KeepStoredURL` maps any exact echo of the redaction back to the stored URL, so a form that sends back what it was shown keeps the row unchanged. A legacy ssh URL with a password is shown without it and cannot be scrubbed by saving: the URL has to change or the password be rotated. The upgrade note is in `docs/upgrades.md`. Fingerprint `internal/repos/service.go:validateURL:userinfo-credential-stored-and-returned-to-viewers-and-projections` |
| 7.104 | Run-output redaction misses a secret printed through `to_yaml`/`to_nice_yaml` (single-quoted) or `urlencode` | done | **Fixed 2026-11-10.** Confirmed, low. `internal/worker/runner/redact.go` `newRedactor` knows the raw, JSON and Python-repr spellings, not YAML's `''` doubling or percent-encoding. **Fix:** add the YAML single-quoted body and the two percent-encoded bodies (Python `quote(safe='/')` and `url.QueryEscape`), each with its JSON forms, when they differ from the value. **Done:** `9be5ef39`. As proposed: the single-quoted YAML body (every `'` doubled) and the two percent-encoded bodies (Python `quote(safe='/')` and `url.QueryEscape`), each with its JSON forms. Fingerprint `internal/worker/runner/redact.go:newRedactor:yaml-single-quote-and-urlencode-spellings` |
| 7.105 | The requirements source check skips values the Go YAML parser reads as numbers but ansible-galaxy reads as paths | done | **Fixed 2026-11-10.** Confirmed, low. `internal/worker/requirements` records a role `src`/name or collection name/source as a source only when yaml.v3 tags it `!!str`; `1e3` or `0o17` slip past `checkSources` while galaxy's YAML 1.1 loader reads them as relative paths, so a local source outside the clone is installed. **Fix:** refuse those fields when their tag is neither `!!str` nor `!!null` (the rule `decodePlain` already applies to YAML the two loaders read differently). **Done:** `caf88cc4`. As proposed: a file whose role `src`, role name used as the source, or collection name or source is a scalar that is neither a string nor null is refused as not a requirements file (`notRequirements`); the quoted spelling still plans and is checked. Fingerprint `pulsix/worker/requirements/plan-sources-skip-non-string-scalars` |
| 7.106 | The worker encodes the whole variable-sources report before applying its 8 MiB cap | done | **Fixed 2026-11-10.** Confirmed, low. `internal/varsource/report.go` `Encode` marshals everything, then trims; a ~66 KB repository of aliased playbooks drives a sync to gigabytes and kills the worker. **Fix:** size each playbook on its own, keep the longest prefix that fits, never marshal more than the cap plus one playbook; regression test on allocations. **Done:** `c3e3167a`, `187d17ed`. As proposed: `Encode` encodes playbooks one by one into a buffer that cannot pass the cap, stops at the first that does not fit and splices the envelope around the kept prefix; the allocation test fails at once past 16 caps and carries a memory limit. Fingerprint `internal/varsource/report.go:Encode:full-marshal-before-size-cap` |
| 7.107 | Release guard and registry credentials sit only in workflow code the dispatched ref supplies (no environment since 38098bdb) | done | Lead; the same root cause as 7.37, reopened by the switch to the shared workflow. **Fixed 2026-11-10** with 7.37 (see there): the shared release workflows take an `environment` input and Pulsix passes `release`. The credentials themselves stay organization secrets (owner decision 2026-11-09). Fingerprint `github-workflows/release.yml:registry-secrets-reachable-outside-release-guard` |
| 7.108 | The shared release workflow runs its own actions from `@main` | won't do | Lead. `compute-version@main` and `announce-release@main` run inside the release job. **Owner decision 2026-11-10:** shared workflows and their actions are referenced by `@main` (or a tag) on purpose, callers included, so a shared change needs no pin bump in every product; what guards it is the `main` ruleset of NoiXdev/github-workflows, under which only organization admins may update the branch. Fingerprint `github-workflows/create_docker_changelog_version_release:floating-self-reference-in-pinned-release-workflow` |
| 7.109 | A passkey or TOTP enrolment started before an admin's second-factor reset can finish after it | done | **Fixed 2026-11-10.** Lead (needs a database test). The finish handlers act on the principal resolved when the request arrived; `MarkMFAComplete` and the enrolment confirm do not check that the session still exists. **Fix:** the enrolment finish writes and `MarkMFAComplete` refuse when the session row is gone (the reset deletes the account's sessions), in the same transaction; database test with a stale principal. **Done:** `2cc98c92`, `a832b989`, `494517af`. Differs from the proposed **Fix:** in how: `Sessions.LockLive` takes the account row in key-share mode (the reset takes it `FOR UPDATE`) and then re-checks the session, so enrolment and reset serialise; `Enrolment.FinishPasskey` and `ConfirmTOTP` use it, and `MarkSessionMFAComplete` no longer matches a revoked or expired session. The lock is `FOR KEY SHARE` because `FOR SHARE` deadlocked two concurrent enrolments of one account against the `last_sign_in_at` stamp (round 1). It also covers variant (b), the passkey login (Task 13): `Passkeys.FinishLogin` advances the counter and opens the session in one transaction after locking the account row and re-reading the credential, so a login validated before a reset opens no session for the removed passkey; and the TOTP enrolment on an ended session (`Enrolment.BeginTOTP`, 401). A session that ended mid-request answers 401, not 500. Fingerprint `pulsix/auth/enrolment-writes-not-revalidated-against-ResetSecondFactor` |
| 7.110 | A host agent can store multi-MiB check labels and error texts | done | **Fixed 2026-11-10.** Lead. `internal/checks/samples.go` `withinContract` bounds counts, not the bytes of `label` and `error`, which never expire and are reloaded on every evaluation. **Fix:** `MaxLabelLen` and `MaxErrLen` in `withinContract` (an over-long error is cut, an over-long label refused), matching what the agent's plugins produce. **Done:** `1baa4b69`. As proposed: `MaxLabelLen` (512) refuses the batch, `MaxErrLen` (2048) cuts the error on a UTF-8 boundary so the failing state still records; the agent's `errObservation` cuts at the same bound first. Fingerprint `internal/checks/samples.go:withinContract:unbounded-label-and-error-bytes-per-tuple` |
| 7.111 | One agent identity can send maximum check-result batches back to back | done | **Fixed 2026-11-10.** Lead (needs a load measurement). `internal/agentapi/stream.go` `receiveMessages` accepts batches as fast as they come, each one transaction on the instance-wide pool. **Fix:** a per-stream budget on check-result observations per interval, generous enough for the shortest check interval, with excess batches dropped and counted. **Done:** `266e2e32`, `0cc7b53b`. Differs from the proposed **Fix:** the budget is paced per agent serial across all its streams (kept in the `StreamRegistry`, like the resolve limit of 7.25), not per stream, because a reconnect would buy a fresh burst; and an agent over budget is delayed, not dropped, because dropping lost the agent's legitimate replay after an outage. The receive loop waits (cancellable) until the bucket holds the batch, refreshing `last_seen` once per heartbeat interval; the bucket refills 4096 observations per `checks.MinInterval` (now exported) and bursts at 8192. A batch over `checks.MaxObservationsPerBatch` is refused before it is charged. The throttle is only logged (once a minute per stream); see 3.27. Fingerprint `internal/agentapi/stream.go:receiveMessages:no-per-agent-check-results-ingest-budget` |
| 7.112 | A host whose evaluation outlasts the batch deadline is never postponed and leads every evaluation claim | done | **Fixed 2026-11-10.** Lead (needs a database test). The 7.58 retry backoff applies to a host that failed, not to the one the batch deadline cut off, so it heads the next claim for every organization. **Decision:** the host running when the deadline fires gets the same `retry_after` backoff, and `ContextForHost` runs under the per-host deadline. A failure under a deadline (the host's own `hostEvalDeadline` of 30 s around the whole evaluation, or the batch's) postpones the host on a context detached from the batch (5 s bound); only a cancelled manager (shutdown) leaves it as it is. Fingerprint `internal/rules/due_sql.go:evaluateOne:deadline-cut-host-never-postponed-heads-shared-eval-claim` |
| 7.113 | One playbook walk keeps every parsed role spec tree alive | done | **Fixed 2026-11-10.** Lead (needs a memory measurement). `internal/varsource/roles.go` keeps each role's parsed tree for the whole walk, about 85× its raw bytes. **Fix:** keep only the extracted results per role and drop the tree once visited. **Done:** `9b70dcdb`, `65624faa`, `2e3dc5c3`, `f2b00e05`, `10c1c014`, `d30afb9c`. Differs from the proposed **Fix:** the first design (map every entry point at parse time, keep the results) opened new amplifications through four review rounds and was replaced. Entry points are mapped lazily, one per use, as before; a spec file's tree and caches are released right after the use that parsed it, and a use of another entry point of the same file re-parses it, charged against the walk's budgets (raw bytes, steps, scalar bytes). Duplicate-key notes already raised are known by the mapping's number in document order, kept as one bit per mapping (under 0.7 MiB for the review shape), so a re-parse does not note them twice. The heap held at the last role drops from 400 MiB to under 0.5 MiB for the duplicate-key shapes and from 85 MiB to 1.5 MiB for 500 roles sharing an aliased options mapping. Fingerprint `internal/varsource/roles.go:walk.spec:parse-trees-retained-across-walk` |
| 7.114 | Editor playbooks' variable-source results are up to 12× the playbook text and every list route loads them all | done | **Fixed 2026-11-10.** Lead (needs a database measurement). `internal/playbooks/varsource.go` `inlineSource` stores an unbounded parse result, and the playbook and template lists decode every one. **Fix:** cap the stored result like the worker's report cap (left-out playbooks marked incomplete) and leave it out of list responses. **Done:** `9b2c365b`, `dc5a9cbc`, `9b65cc63`. The stored result is a one-playbook report encoded by `varsource.Encode` under a 1 MiB cap (`MaxInlineSourceBytes`); one that does not fit is stored as incomplete with the playbook left out. `ListPlaybooks` no longer selects `var_source`. Differs from the proposed **Fix:** the job-template list still reads each linked playbook's source for its review badges (`variables_review`; the SPA uses them and no column can carry the field-level comparison), but one at a time and bounded at 1 MiB. Rows saved before the cap hold the bare playbook and are not decoded when longer than twice the cap (jsonb re-renders with a space after every `:` and `,`, which at most doubles the text, so a row stored under the cap always reads back): they read as incomplete and left out until the playbook is saved again; no migration. A stored review summary is 7.117. Fingerprint `internal/playbooks/varsource.go:inlineSource:inline-parse-result-unbounded-and-loaded-per-list` |
| 7.115 | A NUL byte in an agent's check label or error text makes Postgres reject the whole batch | done | **Done 2026-11-10.** An error text now loses its NUL bytes before the cut (`cleanErr`), and `withinContract` refuses a label holding one, so a batch with a NUL error stores every observation. Found by 7.110's review (2026-11-10); pre-existing. `internal/checks/samples.go` `withinContract` refuses control characters (NUL included) in `check_key`, `item` and `metric` through `breaksLine`, but a `label` and an `error` pass unchecked (gRPC only guarantees valid UTF-8, and NUL is valid), and `internal/agent/results_filter.go` neither drops nor strips it on the agent side. The text then reaches the `text` columns of `check_samples`, `check_current` and `check_states`, which Postgres cannot hold with a 0x00 byte (not reproduced against a database here), so `RecordObservations` fails for the whole batch with a plain error: the stream ends as `Internal`, not as the agent's fault, and every other observation of that batch is lost with it. Real agents do not produce it today (labels are a fixed set, error texts quote OS faults). **Proposal:** strip or replace NUL in `cutErr`'s path for errors and refuse a label holding one in `withinContract`, with a test that a batch holding a NUL error still stores its other observations. Fingerprint `internal/checks/samples.go:withinContract:nul-byte-in-label-or-error-fails-whole-batch` |
| 7.116 | Deleting the passkey a concurrent login uses still opens that login's session | done | **Done 2026-11-10.** `Enrolment.DeletePasskey` and `Enrolment.DisableTOTP` run the last-second-factor check and the removal in one instance transaction that first takes the account row through the new `Sessions.LockAccountForRemoval` (query `LockUserForFactorRemoval`), and the passkey delete and TOTP disable handlers call them instead of the unlocked `SecondFactors` methods; database tests cover a login queued behind a deletion (it opens no session), a deletion queued behind a login, and two concurrent removals of the account's last two factors (exactly one is refused with `ErrLastSecondFactor`). Differs from the **Proposal:** in the lock: it is `FOR UPDATE`, the lock the administrator's reset takes, not the key-share `Sessions.LockAccount`, because two key-share locks do not conflict and a removal holding one would not serialise with a passkey login holding one too; `DisableTOTP` gets the same treatment since its last-factor check shared the same unlocked read. Found by 7.109's Task 13 review (2026-11-10), low. `SecondFactors.DeletePasskey` (`internal/auth/second_factor.go`) is a plain read-then-delete with no transaction and no `users` row lock, so it does not serialise with `Passkeys.FinishLogin`, which since 7.109 locks the account row `FOR KEY SHARE` and then re-reads the credential: a login that re-read the credential just before the delete commits still opens its session for a passkey the owner has just removed. The administrator's second-factor reset is covered (it takes the row `FOR UPDATE`); the owner's own delete is not, and its last-second-factor check shares the same unlocked read. **Proposal:** run `DeletePasskey` in an instance transaction that takes the account row first (`Sessions.LockAccount`, the order enrolment uses), then the checks and the delete; database test with a concurrent login. Fingerprint `internal/auth/second_factor.go:DeletePasskey:no-account-lock-against-concurrent-passkey-login` |
| 7.117 | The job-template list could read a stored review summary instead of each playbook's source | idea | Follow-up of 7.114 (optional performance). The list still reads each linked playbook's stored source for the `variables_review` badges, one at a time and bounded at 1 MiB, so a list costs up to distinct linked playbooks × 1 MiB of database reads. Storing a small review summary (the per-variable facts the badges compare) next to the source when a playbook is saved or synced would make the list independent of the source size; it needs the review to be computable from a summary, which the field-level comparison does not allow today, and an additive column. Measure first: only worth it for an organization with many large editor playbooks |
| 7.118 | The TOTP verify step locks the session row before the account row, the reverse of the second-factor reset | done | **Done 2026-11-10.** `/auth/totp/verify` completes the session through the new `Enrolment.CompleteVerifiedSession`, which takes the account row first (`Sessions.LockLive`, key share) and then runs `MarkMFAComplete` in one instance transaction, so a verify that meets a reset queues behind it and finds its session ended (`ErrNotFound`) instead of deadlocking; `TestVerifiedSessionCompletionAndSecondFactorResetSerialise` reproduces the deadlock (40P01) under the old order. The code is still verified before and outside that transaction, because a rollback would refund the factor attempt a wrong code spends (7.26; `TestTOTPVerifyWithAWrongCodeStillSpendsAnAttempt`). Found by the final review of `fix/audit-run-4` (2026-11-10, M4); pre-existing. `/auth/totp/verify` runs `MarkSessionMFAComplete`, which locks the session row and then updates `users` (`last_sign_in_at`); `ResetSecondFactor` and the reset-link completion lock `users` and then update `sessions`. When they meet, the deadlock detector aborts one of them: a 500 on verify, or a failed administrator reset that has to be retried. It is the one users/sessions path that does not follow the order 7.109 established. **Proposal:** take the account row first (`LockUserForEnrolment`, key-share) in a transaction around verify's `MarkMFAComplete`; database test with a concurrent reset. Fingerprint `internal/auth/session.go:MarkMFAComplete:session-then-user-lock-order-against-reset` |
| 7.119 | Test gaps left by the 7.109 passkey-login fix | done | **Done 2026-11-10.** `TestPasskeyLoginCreatesItsSessionUnderTheAccountLock` (a hook on `Sessions.Create` proves, from a second connection, that the account row is held against `FOR UPDATE NOWAIT`), the API test `TestTOTPEnrolOnASessionEndedByResetIs401` (a revoked cookie gets 401 at enrol) and `TestEnrolRefusalForAnEndedSessionMapsTo401` (the error `BeginTOTP` returns for an ended session maps to 401) now pin both gaps. Found by the final review (Task 13). (1) No test pins that the session insert of `Passkeys.FinishLogin` runs inside the transaction that holds the account lock; a regression that creates the session outside it would stay green. An idea is a hook on `Sessions.Create` that fails the test when it runs without the lock. (2) No API test that `POST /auth/totp/enrol` on a session an administrator's reset has ended answers 401 (`Enrolment.BeginTOTP` is tested, the handler mapping is not) |
| 7.120 | No API-level test of a projection's or a shared definition's URL echo | done | **Done 2026-11-10.** `TestProjectionUpdateEchoingRedactedURLKeepsIt` and `TestSharedDefinitionPutEchoingRedactedURLKeepsIt` (repo_url_credentials_test.go) now pin the echo at the API. Found by the final review (Task 3, 7.103). The echo rule (`KeepStoredURL`) is tested in the repository and shared-definition services, but no API test sends the redacted URL back in an update of a projection or in `PUT` of a shared definition and checks that the stored URL stays |
| 7.121 | No test of a single role entry point that exceeds the walk's budget on its own | done | **Done 2026-11-10.** `TestOneEntryPointOverTheFileBudget` pins one entry point whose own mapping passes the file's budget on the first read: both uses are unreadable, one file_too_complex note, and the file is opened once (the later entry point does not read it again); no fix was needed. Found by the final review (Task 11, 7.113). The re-parse of a released role spec file is tested against the walk's caps over many entry points, but no test covers one entry point whose own mapping passes the file's budget on its own, so what such a file reports (and that its later uses do not read it again) is not pinned |
| 7.122 | The manager does not enforce the number range on a worker's variable-sources report and does not guard the read length of a stored repository result | done | **Done 2026-11-10.** `repos.scanJSON` now also parses every number outside a string and `decodeReport` refuses a report holding one of 1e21 or more, or below 1e-6 (zero aside), or out of float64's range, against the new `varsource.PlainFloat` the worker's parser shares; `RecordVarSources` logs "variable sources ignored" and keeps the stored record and rows. Ruling: the whole report is refused rather than single playbooks marked unread, since that is the existing path for a malformed report, needs no new note or SPA string, and only a worker older than 390f7248 sends such a number. On the read side `decodePlaybookSource` no longer decodes a stored `result` longer than `maxStoredResultReadBytes` (2× `varsource.MaxReportBytes`, the `maxInlineReadBytes` pattern): it reads as missing with a warn log, never an error. Tests: `TestPlainFloat`, `TestDecodeReportRefusesAnUnplainNumber`, `TestDecodeReportAcceptsWhatTheCurrentWorkerSends` (through the worker's parser and encoder), `TestStoredPlaybookResultOverTheReadLimitReadsAsMissing`. Found by the final review (I2) of `fix/audit-run-4`; pre-existing, see 7.75. Since the fix, the worker's parser no longer turns a number of 1e21 or more, or below 1e-6 (zero aside), into a form value, because jsonb writes such a number out in full and a stored result can grow far past its compact size. A worker that is not upgraded yet can still send them, and `repo_playbook_sources.result` has no read-length guard like the editor rows' `maxInlineReadBytes` (2× the 1 MiB cap). **Proposal:** (1) `repos.decodeReport` already walks the raw bytes in `scanJSON`; let it refuse, or mark as unread, a report holding a number outside [1e-6, 1e21), so the manager no longer depends on the worker's version. (2) Apply the `maxInlineReadBytes` pattern on the read side: a stored result longer than a multiple of the report cap is not decoded and reads as left out. Fingerprint `internal/repos/sources.go:decodeReport:no-number-range-check-on-worker-report` |
| 7.123 | The compose file does not publish the manager's agent port | done | **Done 2026-11-11.** `docker-compose.yml` publishes the endpoint on the host (`PULSIX_GRPC_HOST_PORT`, default 8444) and passes `PULSIX_GRPC_PUBLIC_ADDR` through for a changed host port or name; the comments, `.env.example` and `deploy/compose/README.md` (quick start, "Workers on other machines", the embedded worker's re-attach) say so; `docs/upgrades.md` notes it. Found by the 1.33 docs reconciliation (2026-11-11). Agents connect to the manager directly on `PULSIX_GRPC_LISTEN` (`:8444`; `internal/grpcsrv/auth.go` serves workers and agents on one endpoint, `internal/agentdist/files/install.sh` takes `--manager <manager>:8444`), but `deploy/compose/docker-compose.yml` does not publish 8444 and its comment says nothing outside the compose network needs it by default, which is true only for workers. A host enrolled from the GUI therefore cannot connect to a compose install until the operator publishes the port by hand (the docs page now says so). **Proposal:** publish 8444 by default (host port overridable, like `PULSIX_HOST_PORT`), fix the comment, and document it in `deploy/compose/README.md`. That README is also stale on the embedded worker: it says every manager start registers a new `embedded` worker entry, while `internal/app/embedded_worker.go:33` reuses the same one |

## v1.0 · UI overhaul: host view and its follow-ups

From the owner's first look at the hosts pages on a fresh local stack (2026-11-03). Design: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-03-host-view-design.md`. The host view is the template the other list and detail views follow, using the building blocks in `web/src/ui/detail-layout.tsx` (`DetailLayout`, `SidebarFacts`, `Section`, `DisabledHint`) and `web/src/ui/list-row.tsx` (`ListRow`, `RowMenu`).

| # | Item | Status | Notes |
|---|------|--------|-------|
| 7.65 | Host view overhaul: host page as sidebar and sections, clickable list rows with a working row menu, one add and edit dialog, `Switch` for every boolean setting, runs filtered by host | done | **Done 2026-11-03** on `feat/host-view`. The host page is a sticky sidebar (identity, key facts, capability switches, tags, Run command, Edit, section navigation) beside the sections State, Alerts, Checks, Runs, Connection & SSH, Inventory and Facts; nothing is clipped. A host with monitoring off shows one "Monitoring is off" card instead of State, Alerts and Checks, and one with management off shows "Management is off" instead of Connection & SSH and Inventory. The list row is a link, its menu (Open, Edit, Run command, monitoring and management on or off, Delete) is portalled, and the capabilities are read-only pills. The add and edit dialog sets name, address, zone, jump host (inherited, direct or specific) and tags in one transaction (`POST` and `PATCH /hosts` take the optional `jump: {mode, bastion_id}` and `tags`). `GET /jobs?host_id=` returns the runs that targeted the host (`jobs.host_ids` contains it); the runs page reads `?host=` and shows a removable chip. Migration 00079 adds the GIN index. End-user docs: `noix-docs`, branch `pulsix`. |
| 7.66 | First-run setup requires a setup token | done | **Done 2026-11-03** on `feat/setup-token`. The token is derived from the master key (HKDF-SHA256, 10 bytes, base32, `XXXX-XXXX-XXXX-XXXX`), not stored, so every manager and every restart shows the same value. While setup is open the manager logs the token and a `/setup?token=` link at every start; `pulsix-manager setup-token` (compose: `docker compose run --rm cli setup-token`) prints both. The wizard's first step asks for the token, the link pre-fills it, and a wrong token is a 403 charged against the setup rate limit. A completed instance answers 409 whatever token it is sent. End-user docs: first-setup page in `noix-docs`. |
| 7.67 | Inventory group detail on the host-view building blocks | done | Package `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-05-detail-views.md`, branch `feat/7.67-detail-views` (2026-11-05). The group page is a `DetailLayout`: sidebar (name, complete/incomplete, parent, members, child groups, fields, jump host; Edit group dialog with its own revision, ⋯ Delete group…) and sections Members, Group values, Variable schema, Child groups, Preview inventory; member rows are `ListRow`s to the host with ⋯ Edit values / Remove from group…; each section's draft is keyed by group id and content, so saving one keeps another's draft; old `?tab=` links redirect to the section. The schema builder collapses to two columns on a narrow column (container query) |

No worker page (the API has no single-worker read or per-worker history); worker rows are `StaticListRow`s with ⋯ Revoke… / Delete…, pending enrolments ⋯ Withdraw, labelled apart from workers of the same name |
| 7.69 | Agent list and detail on the host-view building blocks | done | Package `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-05-detail-views.md`, branch `feat/7.67-detail-views` (2026-11-05). No agent page: the host page is the agent's page and keeps its agent card. Agent rows open the host at its Connection section; ⋯ Open host / Revoke…; tokens ⋯ Withdraw |

The run page is a `DetailLayout` (sidebar with status, Cancel run / Retry failed and facts; sections Result, Run group, Vault identities, Event log); the per-host results table scrolls sideways inside its card; runs-list rows are links with ⋯ Open / Cancel run; the host page's run rows open the run |
| 7.71 | Check and rule pages on the host-view building blocks | done | Package `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-05-detail-views.md`, branch `feat/7.67-detail-views` (2026-11-05). No detail pages; checks, rules, monitoring groups and item decisions each have a row file with a ⋯ menu (Edit, Copy for built-in/shared, Delete…); New rule / New decision explain with a hint why they are disabled when no metric is reported. `RuleDialog.tsx`, `HostChecksCard.tsx` and `HostCheckValueRow.tsx` untouched (3.42) |
| 7.72 | Notification channels on the host-view building blocks | done | Package `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-05-detail-views.md`, branch `feat/7.67-detail-views` (2026-11-05). No channel page; rows have ⋯ Deliveries / Send test / Edit / Delete…, Send test shows "Sending test" and is disabled while in flight (`RowMenuItem` gained `disabled`); the no-mail note is a hint above the list. Also: an E2E check that no section or card overflows sideways at 1280×720 on the group, host and run pages |
| 7.86 | Leftovers from the detail-views reviews | done | Package `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-06-7x-followups.md`, branch `fix/7.83-7.87` (2026-11-05). Every item: the viewer schema browsing was already fixed by 7.75 (now tested); `useReloadInventory` fails only on the group's own read; the values form saves on the revision its draft started from once edited (an unedited draft follows the group, and a save rebuilds it with keep markers); Send test is per channel; check menus name their binding; fleet rows are `ListRow`s; `DetailLayout` acts on a hash again on another page; `DisabledHint` is a named note; jsdom `scrollTo` is stubbed (132 warnings → 0). The WebKit container-query item is obsolete (no container query is left). `SchemaSection` has the same lost update: 7.89 |
| 7.73 | Runs host filter: confirm the planner uses the GIN index under the generic plan | done | Package `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-05-7x-leftovers.md`, branch `fix/7.x-leftovers` (2026-11-05). Measured: the planner never uses `jobs_host_ids_idx` for the app role, with a custom or a generic plan. The cause is row-level security, not the plan cache: `@>` (`arraycontains`) is not LEAKPROOF, so under RLS the predicate cannot be pushed into the index; as superuser the same generic plan uses it. A query rewrite or another index cannot fix this; follow-up 7.84. The measuring test is kept for 7.84 |
| 7.74 | Settings as a sidebar, shared resources as pages, a member edit view with account actions, "forgot password", create-organization UI | done | **Done 2026-11-04** on `feat/7.74-settings-and-accounts`. Spec: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-04-settings-and-accounts-design.md`. Settings have a grouped side navigation (Organization: General, Members, Activity, API tokens; Alerting; Instance: Users, Organizations, Outgoing mail, Language, Backups, three shared-resource pages), collapsing into a select below 900 px; every old settings URL still works. Shared repositories, job templates and check definitions have list pages and form pages (`/settings/shared/{repos,templates,checks}`, `…/new`, `…/{id}`) with an unsaved-changes guard; the template page keeps `SchemaBuilder` for 7.75. Instance admins get a Users page and an account page (`/settings/users/{id}`); the member page (`/settings/members/{id}`) replaces the role dialog and shows the account sections to instance admins. Account actions (send reset link, temporary password shown once and valid 24 h, reset second factor, end all sessions, deactivate, reactivate) are instance-admin only, cookie-session only, need the step-up, refuse one's own account (`not_on_own_account`), are audited with org nil, and mail a best-effort notice only to active accounts. Forgot password on the login page (neutral answer, per-client and per-address limiter, 429 shows "too many attempts", "ask an admin" without mail); reset links in the URL fragment, 60 min, single-use, SHA-256 hashed, never sign in and never skip the second factor. `users.must_change_password` gates every cookie session, passkey sign-ins included, with `403 password_change_required` until the forced change at `/account/password`; API tokens are not gated. Account security has a Password card. The password floor is 12 everywhere, invite acceptance included. `POST /orgs` requires an instance admin; "New organization" in the switcher and an instance Organizations page. Migration 00080. | 7.92 | A worker umask of 077 breaks every requirements install | done | Package `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-08-requirements-leftovers.md`, branch `fix/7.92-7.97-requirements-leftovers` (2026-11-08). The setuid helper's exec form sets `umask(old & ~(mode_t)050)` before `execv` (after the privilege drop): it clears exactly the group read and execute bits of the worker's mask and keeps every other bit, so nothing the worker asked for is loosened except group read and traverse (077 becomes 027; 022, 002, 007 and the owner bits stay). Plays and galaxy installs both go through the helper; without a play uid galaxy runs as the worker and the question does not arise. The pinned test flipped to the install's assertions (`TestRequirementsUnderAWorkerUmaskOf077`), and `TestSetuidHelperFloorsTheUmask` pins the rows, verified in the rebuilt worker image |
| 7.93 | Requirements installs and plays must not overlap under the play uid | superseded | Found by 7.88 (2026-11-06). The take-over cannot detect content another play-uid process writes into the temp entry's 2770 dirs; today the worker loop runs one job at a time (`loop.go` only warns for `PULSIX_MAX_CONCURRENT` > 1), so no play runs during an install. When real concurrency arrives, serialize installs against plays or give installs their own uid (comment at `runner/galaxy.go`). The repo cache sweep (7.95) relies on it too: it runs between jobs, so no run uses a cache it removes. **Superseded 2026-11-09 by 7.100**, which lists it with the other prerequisites of running jobs side by side; until then the one-job loop and the between-jobs play-uid sweep keep it from happening |
| 7.94 | A repository subpath stored with a leading slash breaks its runs | done | Found by 7.88 (2026-11-06), fixed 2026-11-08. The repos service accepted `/ansible`; the manager then sent `subpath: "/ansible/site.yml"`, which the worker's `safeSubpath` refuses, and a sync could not open `/ansible`, `./ansible` or `a//b` as its read root. `repos.CanonicalSubpath` now stores one form (no empty or `.` segments, `""` for the root) for an organization's repository and an installation's definition alike, the definition gaining the repository's refusals (`..`, characters, 200 characters); migration 00083 rewrites the stored values. The worker stays strict: after the migration no manager sends another form |
| 7.95 | A deleted repository's clone and installs stay on every worker | done | Found while specifying 7.88 (2026-11-06). Nothing removes `<org>/<repo>` or `<repo>.requirements/` from a worker's cache after the repository is deleted. Sweep caches of repositories the worker no longer knows. **Done 2026-11-09:** new WorkerService call `ListRepositories` (`internal/grpcsrv/repos.go`) answers the authenticated worker's organization and its repository ids. The worker (`internal/worker/cachesweep.go`) asks at start and then every six hours between jobs (five minutes after a failed call), and removes `<repo>`, `<repo>.requirements` and `<repo>.galaxy` of every unlisted repository plus other organizations' directories: real directories with a canonical uuid name only, renamed to `.removing-*` first, cleared as the play uid where the worker alone cannot (then removed by the worker whatever the play uid's pass reported). No valid answer (an older manager's `Unimplemented`, a failed call, an unreadable id) removes nothing. A manager-side delete job was rejected: one worker would claim it and an offline worker would miss it. The sweep relies on the loop running one job at a time; 7.93's concurrency has to keep it off running jobs' caches |
| 7.96 | Roles inside installed collections are not read | done | Found while specifying 7.88 (2026-11-06). The variable read reports `ns.col.role` as `role_collection`; with collections now installed per commit it could read them from the entry's `collections/`, following the play's `collections:` search list and the playbook-adjacent `collections/`. From 7.91: ansible-playbook tries a dotted three-part role name (`ns.col.role`) as a collection role before the role search (`RoleDefinition._load_role_path`, ansible-core 2.21.5); the parser reports it as `role_collection` until this item reads collection roles. **Done 2026-11-09:** the variable read looks a role up as a collection role first, as `RoleDefinition._load_role_path` does (checked against ansible-playbook 2.21.5 `--syntax-check`): a fully qualified name (`ns.col[.sub].role`) in its own collection only, a short name in each collection of the `collections:` list that applies to the use (the play's for `roles:`; for role tasks the nearest of task, enclosing blocks and play, an empty list too, and a block's null value empties it while a task's keeps the inherited one; a `roles:` entry's own `collections` key is not one; `ansible.builtin` is never on disk). Each collection tried costs the walk a step and each collection is looked for once per walk, so a long list ends in the walk's limits. A collection is the first of `<top playbook dir>/collections/ansible_collections` (only the playbook a run starts with; ansible-playbook adds no imported playbook's) and the installed `collections/ansible_collections` that has `<ns>/<col>` as a directory; the role is `<collection>/roles/<path>` as a directory (a dotted short name is a path, `x.y` → `x/y`). Not found there, the role search follows. Installed collection files are shown as `(collections)/…`. A qualified name whose collection is in neither place stays the hint `role_collection` (the worker's own collections path may hold it), and so does a short name found nowhere whose list names such a collection; one whose collection exists but lacks the role is `role_not_found`, and so is a dotted name that is no valid collection reference (`my-ns.col.role`), which used to be `role_collection`. A `collections:` value that is no name or list of names is the new warning `collections_invalid`. Code: `internal/varsource/roles_collections.go`, the sync's `openInstalledFS` (`internal/worker/clonefs.go`) |
| 7.97 | Leftovers from the 7.88 reviews | done | Package `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-11-08-requirements-leftovers.md`, branch `fix/7.92-7.97-requirements-leftovers` (2026-11-08). **Checks:** a source below a directory the worker cannot read says "cannot be read: permission denied" and a glob character says so, instead of "outside the repository" (both still refused); every `hg+` source and `scm: hg` (also on a `role:` mapping, whatever its `src`) and every collection named or sourced `hg+…` is refused with "Mercurial sources are not supported", where `scm: hg` next to a github http `src` and the collection `hg+` forms are deliberate over-refusals (conservative, and the image has no `hg`); `walkTree` reads a directory in batches of `listChunk` and charges the entry budget per batch, so one huge directory fails by name; an untyped `ns.class` stays accepted (P1: galaxy reads a Python keyword as a local path, our check walks it as one and as a name, so no reading escapes it), recorded in `checkUntypedName` and pinned by a test, and harmless git/subdirs collection names stay refused; Python `file:line` citations became galaxy function names, read against ansible-core 2.21.5. **Runtime:** a token used as a bare URL username is redacted in galaxy output, log lines and failure tails through the new leaf package `internal/worker/urlcred`, shared by `requirements` and `runner` (P3 consequence: `ssh://user@host` URLs now lose their user too, `ssh://[redacted]@host/…`, while scp-style `git@host:` stays); a 64 KiB galaxy line is never cut inside a word and the 16 KiB stderr tail starts at a word; a fetch or `HeadCommit` cancelled mid-git ends cancelled, not error (P5: `runGitOut` reports a git its context ended as stopped, so a run or sync cancelled during the fetch also ends cancelled). **Tests:** the take-over's changed-while-copied and destination branches are covered through two new seams (`beforeOpen`, `afterRead`); the in-image warning filter catches the stop-path warnings and the entry walk checks exact owner and group modes; `TarGz`, `RoleTarball` and `EvilCollectionTarball` live once in `requirementstest`; `worker.CleanRepoPath` replaces `cleanSubpath` and `runner.cleanRepoPath`; `internal/e2e/uidboundary/requirements_test.go` is split by topic. **Won't fix:** YAML 1.1 implicit types differ from yaml.v3 only for non-strings and have no escape route; a hard link to the worker key whose original is removed during an install needs `protected_hardlinks=0`, and the kernel default protects against it; `removeEntry`'s temp-name claim is Lstat-then-Rename, a race with probability 2^-64 |
| 7.98 | A credential with an unencoded `/`, `?` or `#` in a URL's userinfo is not redacted | done | Found by the 7.97 review (2026-11-08), fixed 2026-11-08 (owner's choice B). `urlcred.Redact`'s rules stopped the userinfo at those characters, so `https://user:ab/cd@host`, a CodeCommit-style password or a base64 token as a bare username (`https://abc/def+g==@host`) was left as it was in galaxy output, failure tails and git diagnostics. A URL with a scheme now loses everything from `://` to the last `@` before the next white space, and an scp-style `user:password@` may hold a `/` (not as its first character). Accepted over-redaction: a URL with an `@` in its path or query loses its host too (`https://registry/@scope/pkg` → `https://[redacted]@scope/pkg`), and so does everything up to the last `@` in the same run of text without white space (compact JSON, comma-joined URLs); `see:https://tok@h` becomes `[redacted]@h`. A match still holds no white space, so the galaxy line splitter never cuts one |
| 7.99 | A role's own `meta/requirements.yml` dependencies are not checked before galaxy installs them | won't do | Found by the 7.97 review (2026-11-08). Galaxy parses them through `RoleRequirement.role_yaml_parse`, so a dependency could name `scm: hg` or a local path. These are remote role data, like a collection's dependencies, which cannot be checked before the download; the play-uid install confines them. **Closed 2026-11-09 as an accepted limit** (owner's choice A): like a collection's dependencies, they are remote data the worker cannot see before galaxy downloads the role, and the install runs as the play uid, so it reads and writes only what that uid may. Checking them would mean installing with `--no-deps` and re-implementing galaxy's dependency resolution (versions, duplicates, roles already installed), more new failure modes than it removes |
| 7.100 | Running jobs side by side (`PULSIX_MAX_CONCURRENT` above 1) | idea | Collected 2026-11-09 from 7.93 and the notes of 7.88 and 7.95. Today the loop claims the next job only after the last one reported (`internal/worker/loop.go`; a `PULSIX_MAX_CONCURRENT` above 1 only logs a warning), and several worker parts rely on that. Each has to change before jobs may overlap: (1) **requirements installs and plays under the play uid** (was 7.93): a play could write into an install's temp entry (2770 dirs) unnoticed by the take-over, so serialize installs against plays or give installs a uid of their own (`runner/galaxy.go`, `Galaxy.Install`); (2) **the between-jobs play-uid sweep** (7.21, 7.35; `Loop.runOne`'s deferred `sweepPlayUID`) ends every process of the play uid, so it would end other jobs' plays: it needs per-job process tracking or a uid per job; (3) **a repository's clone** is fetched and reset without a lock (spec `2026-11-06-requirements-cache-design.md`, "Lock scope"): a run reading it while a sync resets it sees a mixed tree, so the clone needs the repository's lock; (4) **requirements eviction** must skip entries a running job uses (same spec); (5) **the repo cache sweep** (7.95, `internal/worker/cachesweep.go`) runs between jobs and must not remove a cache a running job uses. Each job already has its own work dir |

## Decisions (short)

- Stack: Go (manager, worker, agent), React/TypeScript SPA, PostgreSQL + TimescaleDB, gRPC over mTLS, no broker.
- **One installation per service provider.** A provider runs their own Pulsix; their customers are organizations inside it. There is deliberately no provider entity above the organization, and resources shared across customers belong to the *installation* — the shape `instance_settings` and `instance_ca` already have. Rejected: a provider level on one shared installation. It would make every policy, role, query and navigation path carry a table with one row in the common case, and it would replace physical separation (own database, own process, own master key) with a policy predicate — a step down for a product that holds other people's SSH keys. If a multi-provider installation is ever needed, the path is a mechanical migration: add `provider_id`, backfill every instance-level row to the single provider, widen the policies. Decided 2026-10-19.
- Multi-tenant and self-hostable. Manager HA, workers per zone.
- Host capabilities `managed` (Ansible via SSH) and `monitored` (agent and/or remote checks) are independent.
- Inventory groups (Ansible, schema builder) and monitoring groups (tag selectors, alerting) are separate concepts.
- Agent config is hybrid: checks from GUI, secrets local only.
- One version and one tag for manager, worker and agent; manager accepts same and previous minor version.
- Check engine layered like Checkmk/Zabbix: plugins in code, definitions and rules as data, inheritance plugin → org → group → host → item, discovery, custom checks in Nagios or JSON format.
- Status colours (ok/warn/crit/unknown) are reserved for states; cyan is the action colour.
- GitHub repo `NoiXdev/pulsix` starts **private**; goes public with the v1.0 launch.
- Test policy: every feature ships with tests; E2E scenarios (agent, monitoring, Ansible, HA, auth, tenancy, status pages) are milestone gates; coverage enforced in CI.
